- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/DefaultProcessRunnerTest.java` (as amended by the Task 11 quality-review fixes):
```java
package io.joeyang.tracer.cli;

import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertEquals;
import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertThrows;
import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertTrue;

import java.nio.file.Path;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.Arrays;
import java.util.List;
import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test;
import org.junit.jupiter.api.io.TempDir;

class DefaultProcessRunnerTest {
    final ProcessRunner runner = new DefaultProcessRunner();
    @TempDir Path tmp;

    @Test void capturesExitCodeStdoutAndStderr() throws Exception {
        var r = runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(10), List.of("sh", "-c", "echo out; echo err 1>&2; exit 3"));
        assertEquals(3, r.exitCode());
        assertEquals("out\n", r.stdout());
        assertEquals("err\n", r.stderr());
    }

    @Test void runsInTheGivenDirectory() throws Exception {
        var r = runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(10), List.of("pwd"));
        assertEquals(tmp.toRealPath().toString(), r.stdout().strip());
    }

    @Test void missingBinaryCannotStart() {
        ProcessException e = assertThrows(ProcessException.class,
                () -> runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(10), List.of("definitely-not-a-binary-xyz", "--version")));
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("cannot start 'definitely-not-a-binary-xyz'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void timeoutKillsTheProcessAndReportsIt() {
        long start = System.nanoTime();
        ProcessException e = assertThrows(ProcessException.class,
                () -> runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofMillis(300), List.of("sh", "-c", "sleep 30")));
        long elapsedMs = (System.nanoTime() - start) / 1_000_000;
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("did not finish"), e.getMessage());
        assertTrue(elapsedMs < 5_000, "took " + elapsedMs + "ms; the child must be killed, not awaited");
    }

    @Test void largeOutputDoesNotDeadlock() throws Exception {
        var r = runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(30), List.of("sh", "-c",
                "head -c 3000000 /dev/zero | tr '\\0' x; head -c 2000000 /dev/zero | tr '\\0' y 1>&2"));
        assertEquals(0, r.exitCode());
        assertEquals(3_000_000, r.stdout().length());
        assertEquals(2_000_000, r.stderr().length());
    }

    @Test void nullCwdIsRejected() {
        assertThrows(NullPointerException.class,
                () -> runner.run(null, Duration.ofSeconds(1), List.of("pwd")));
    }

    @Test void nullTimeoutIsRejected() {
        assertThrows(NullPointerException.class,
                () -> runner.run(tmp, null, List.of("pwd")));
    }

    @Test void nullCommandIsRejected() {
        assertThrows(NullPointerException.class,
                () -> runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(1), null));
    }

    @Test void emptyCommandIsRejected() {
        assertThrows(IllegalArgumentException.class,
                () -> runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(1), List.of()));
    }

    @Test void nonPositiveTimeoutIsRejected() {
        assertThrows(IllegalArgumentException.class,
                () -> runner.run(tmp, Duration.ZERO, List.of("pwd")));
        assertThrows(IllegalArgumentException.class,
                () -> runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(-1), List.of("pwd")));
    }

    @Test void aBackgroundOrphanDoesNotExtendTheWaitPastTheDeadline() {
        long start = System.nanoTime();
        ProcessException e = assertThrows(ProcessException.class,
                () -> runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(2),
                        List.of("sh", "-c", "sleep 4 & sleep 0.5; echo hi")));
        long elapsedMs = (System.nanoTime() - start) / 1_000_000;
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("did not finish"), e.getMessage());
        assertTrue(elapsedMs < 3_500,
                "took " + elapsedMs + "ms; a backgrounded grandchild holding the pipe open must not extend the wait to 4s");
    }

    @Test void hugeTimeoutDoesNotOverflow() throws Exception {
        var r = runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(Long.MAX_VALUE), List.of("pwd"));
        assertEquals(0, r.exitCode());
    }

    @Test void commandReadingStdinReturnsPromptlyBecauseStdinIsClosed() throws Exception {
        long start = System.nanoTime();
        var r = runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(5), List.of("cat"));
        long elapsedMs = (System.nanoTime() - start) / 1_000_000;
        assertEquals(0, r.exitCode());
        assertEquals("", r.stdout());
        assertTrue(elapsedMs < 2_000, "took " + elapsedMs + "ms; cat must see immediate EOF on stdin, not hang");
    }

    @Test void drainsRunOnNamedTracerThreadsRatherThanTheCommonPool() throws Exception {
        Thread caller = new Thread(() -> {
            try {
                runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(5), List.of("sh", "-c", "sleep 1"));
            } catch (ProcessException ignored) {
                // only the thread names while in flight matter for this test
            }
        });
        caller.start();
        boolean sawNamedDrainThread = false;
        long pollUntil = System.nanoTime() + Duration.ofSeconds(3).toNanos();
        while (System.nanoTime() < pollUntil && !sawNamedDrainThread) {
            sawNamedDrainThread = Thread.getAllStackTraces().keySet().stream()
                    .anyMatch(t -> t.getName().startsWith("tracer-stdout") || t.getName().startsWith("tracer-stderr"));
            if (!sawNamedDrainThread) {
                Thread.sleep(20);
            }
        }
        caller.join();
        assertTrue(sawNamedDrainThread,
                "expected a thread named tracer-stdout/tracer-stderr while a run() was draining output");
    }

    @Test void timeoutKillsTheEntireProcessTreeNotJustTheShell() throws Exception {
        String marker = uniqueMarker("41"); // unique per run so this test cannot match an unrelated process
        ProcessException e = assertThrows(ProcessException.class,
                () -> runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(1), List.of("sh", "-c", "sleep " + marker + " & wait")));
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("did not finish"), e.getMessage());
        assertTrue(waitForNoProcessMatching(marker),
                "expected the backgrounded 'sleep " + marker + "' to be killed along with the shell");
    }

    @Test void preInterruptedThreadThrowsInterruptedAndKillsTheChild() throws Exception {
        String marker = uniqueMarker("52"); // unique per run so this test cannot match an unrelated process
        Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
        try {
            ProcessException e = assertThrows(ProcessException.class,
                    () -> runner.run(tmp, Duration.ofSeconds(30), List.of("sleep", marker)));
            assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("interrupted"), e.getMessage());
        } finally {
            assertTrue(Thread.interrupted(), "expected the interrupt flag to still be set after run() propagated it");
        }
        assertTrue(waitForNoProcessMatching(marker),
                "expected 'sleep " + marker + "' to be killed after the interrupt");
    }

    /**
     * A sleep duration (one decimal point, so it stays valid for {@code sleep}) that is unique to this JVM and this
     * call, so it cannot collide with an unrelated process already on the (possibly shared, e.g. Bazel sandbox) host.
     */
    private static String uniqueMarker(String wholeSeconds) {
        return wholeSeconds + "." + ProcessHandle.current().pid() + System.nanoTime();
    }

    /** Polls the live process table for a process with an exact argument of {@code marker}, up to 10s. */
    private static boolean waitForNoProcessMatching(String marker) throws InterruptedException {
        long deadline = System.nanoTime() + Duration.ofSeconds(10).toNanos();
        boolean dead;
        do {
            dead = ProcessHandle.allProcesses()
                    .noneMatch(p -> p.info().arguments().map(Arrays::asList).map(args -> args.contains(marker)).orElse(false));
            if (!dead) {
                Thread.sleep(50);
            }
        } while (!dead && System.nanoTime() < deadline);
        return dead;
    }
}
```

(The tree-kill and interrupt tests use a unique numeric "marker" embedded in the sleep duration argument and scan
`ProcessHandle.allProcesses()` for it, rather than reading a pid from a file: a pidfile-based approach that writes
after forking a background job races against how fast `killTree` runs, especially for the pre-interrupted case
where the kill can happen before the shell has had a chance to write anything. The marker approach only depends on
the process already being live in the OS process table, which is guaranteed the instant `ProcessBuilder.start()`
returns, independent of any race inside the child's shell script. This test was observed to need meaningfully more
than 5s to confirm death when the host machine is heavily loaded by many concurrent sandboxed test actions; the
poll window was set to 10s for that reason. A second Task 11 quality-review round traced those >5s sightings to a
fixed literal marker colliding with an unrelated `sleep` already on the host — the Bazel sandbox shares the host's
process table, so another process's command line could contain the same literal substring. The fix, in a later
quality-review commit, builds the marker per run from the JVM's own pid and `System.nanoTime()` and matches an exact
entry of `info().arguments()` instead of a `contains` on the whole command line; the kill itself was always visible
within tens of milliseconds once collisions were ruled out, so the 10s window stayed only as headroom, not because
the kill is actually slow.)

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/DefaultProcessRunner.java` (as amended by the Task 11 quality-review fixes — see below for why):
```java
package io.joeyang.tracer.cli;

import java.io.File;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.io.InputStream;
import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import java.time.DateTimeException;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.time.Instant;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Objects;
import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;
import java.util.concurrent.ExecutionException;
import java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit;
import java.util.concurrent.TimeoutException;
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;

/** Runs commands with {@link ProcessBuilder}; streams are drained concurrently so large output cannot deadlock. */
public final class DefaultProcessRunner implements ProcessRunner {

    private static final AtomicInteger STDOUT_COUNT = new AtomicInteger();
    private static final AtomicInteger STDERR_COUNT = new AtomicInteger();

    @Override
    public Result run(Path cwd, Duration timeout, List<String> command) throws ProcessException {
        Objects.requireNonNull(cwd, "cwd must not be null");
        Objects.requireNonNull(timeout, "timeout must not be null");
        Objects.requireNonNull(command, "command must not be null");
        if (command.isEmpty()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("command must not be empty");
        }
        if (timeout.isZero() || timeout.isNegative()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("timeout must be positive, got " + timeout);
        }

        Instant deadline = safeDeadline(timeout);
        Process process;
        try {
            process = new ProcessBuilder(command)
                    .directory(cwd.toFile())
                    .redirectInput(ProcessBuilder.Redirect.from(new File("/dev/null")))
                    .start();
        } catch (IOException e) {
            throw ProcessException.cannotStart(command, e);
        }
        CompletableFuture<String> stdout = drain(process.getInputStream(), "tracer-stdout-" + STDOUT_COUNT.incrementAndGet());
        CompletableFuture<String> stderr = drain(process.getErrorStream(), "tracer-stderr-" + STDERR_COUNT.incrementAndGet());
        boolean finished;
        try {
            finished = process.waitFor(safeMillis(timeout), TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            killTree(process);
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
            throw ProcessException.interrupted(command);
        }
        if (!finished) {
            killTree(process);
            throw ProcessException.timeout(command, timeout);
        }

        String out;
        String err;
        try {
            Duration remaining = remainingUntil(deadline);
            CompletableFuture.allOf(stdout, stderr).get(safeMillis(remaining), TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS);
            out = stdout.join();
            err = stderr.join();
        } catch (TimeoutException e) {
            killTree(process);
            throw ProcessException.timeout(command, timeout);
        } catch (ExecutionException e) {
            killTree(process);
            throw asProcessException(command, e.getCause());
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            killTree(process);
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
            throw ProcessException.interrupted(command);
        }
        return new Result(process.exitValue(), out, err);
    }

    /** The budget still left before {@code deadline}; never negative, so a {@code get} call never blocks past it. */
    private static Duration remainingUntil(Instant deadline) {
        try {
            Duration remaining = Duration.between(Instant.now(), deadline);
            return remaining.isNegative() ? Duration.ZERO : remaining;
        } catch (ArithmeticException overflow) {
            return Duration.ofMillis(Long.MAX_VALUE);
        }
    }

    /** {@code Instant.now().plus(timeout)}, clamped instead of overflowing for an absurdly large timeout. */
    private static Instant safeDeadline(Duration timeout) {
        try {
            return Instant.now().plus(timeout);
        } catch (ArithmeticException | DateTimeException overflow) {
            return Instant.MAX;
        }
    }

    /** {@code duration.toMillis()}, clamped instead of overflowing for an absurdly large duration. */
    private static long safeMillis(Duration duration) {
        try {
            return duration.toMillis();
        } catch (ArithmeticException overflow) {
            return Long.MAX_VALUE;
        }
    }

    private static void killTree(Process process) {
        process.descendants().forEach(ProcessHandle::destroyForcibly);
        process.destroyForcibly();
    }

    /**
     * Turns a drain failure into a {@link ProcessException}. An {@link IOException} became {@code ioFailed}; a
     * {@link RuntimeException} or {@link Error} is rethrown as-is rather than being reported as an I/O failure, since
     * it is not one (e.g. it could be an {@link OutOfMemoryError} while buffering a huge stream).
     */
    private static ProcessException asProcessException(List<String> command, Throwable drainFailure) {
        if (drainFailure instanceof IOException io) {
            return ProcessException.ioFailed(command, io);
        }
        if (drainFailure instanceof RuntimeException re) {
            throw re;
        }
        if (drainFailure instanceof Error error) {
            throw error;
        }
        throw new IllegalStateException("unexpected drain failure", drainFailure);
    }

    private static CompletableFuture<String> drain(InputStream in, String threadName) {
        CompletableFuture<String> future = new CompletableFuture<>();
        Thread thread = new Thread(() -> {
            try (in) {
                future.complete(new String(in.readAllBytes(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
            } catch (Throwable t) {
                // Catch Throwable, not just IOException: an uncaught Error (e.g. OutOfMemoryError buffering a huge
                // stream) would otherwise leave this future incomplete forever, so run() would block out its whole
                // timeout budget waiting on it and then misreport a plain timeout instead of the real failure.
                future.completeExceptionally(t);
            }
        }, threadName);
        thread.setDaemon(true);
        thread.start();
        return future;
    }
}
```

(The original implementation shown in the Step 1 draft above used `process.waitFor(timeout.toMillis(), TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)`,
`CompletableFuture.supplyAsync` on the common pool, and `stdout.join()`/`stderr.join()` unconditionally. The Task 11
quality-review rounds hardened it in four commits: (1) input validation, overflow-safe millisecond conversion, and a
single deadline shared across the wait and the drain so a backgrounded grandchild holding a pipe open cannot extend
the call past the caller's timeout, plus `/dev/null` stdin so a command that reads stdin cannot hang; (2) draining on
named daemon threads (`tracer-stdout-N`/`tracer-stderr-N`) instead of the shared common pool, surfacing a failed read
as `ProcessException.ioFailed` instead of silently returning `""`, and fixing the timeout message's duration
formatting; (3) a dedicated process-tree-kill test and a pre-interrupted-thread test; (4) two nits closed in one
commit — the tree-kill/interrupt tests' marker is now built per run from the JVM's pid and `System.nanoTime()`
instead of a fixed literal, to rule out colliding with an unrelated process already on the host (the Bazel sandbox
shares the host's process table), and `drain`'s catch widened from `IOException` to `Throwable` via the
`asProcessException` helper, since catching only `IOException` left an uncaught `Error` (e.g. an `OutOfMemoryError`)
completing nothing — `run()` would then wait out its whole timeout budget and misreport a plain timeout instead of
the real failure. See those commits for the full rationale.)

`tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliOptionsTest.java`:
```java
package io.joeyang.tracer.cli;

import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertEquals;
import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertFalse;
import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertNull;
import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertThrows;
import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertTrue;

import java.nio.file.Path;
import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test;

class CliOptionsTest {
    static Cli.Options parse(String... args) throws UsageException {
        return Cli.Options.parse(args);
    }

    static UsageException fails(String... args) {
        return assertThrows(UsageException.class, () -> parse(args));
    }

    @Test void defaultsForARange() throws Exception {
        Cli.Options o = parse("appA", "--from", "x", "--to", "y");
        assertEquals(":app:appA:compileJava", o.taskPath());
        assertEquals("//app/appA:appA", o.bazelTarget());
        assertTrue(o.bazel());
        assertFalse(o.offline() || o.keepWorktrees() || o.tsv());
        assertNull(o.tsvOut());
        assertNull(o.at());
        assertEquals(10, o.timeout().toMinutes());
    }

    @Test void parsesEveryFlag() throws Exception {
        Cli.Options o = parse("appB", "--from", "x", "--to", "y", "--task", "fingerprint", "--no-bazel", "--offline",
                "--keep-worktrees", "--tsv-out", "rows.tsv", "--timeout-minutes", "3");
        assertEquals(":app:appB:fingerprint", o.taskPath());
        assertFalse(o.bazel());
        assertTrue(o.offline() && o.keepWorktrees());
        assertEquals(Path.of("rows.tsv"), o.tsvOut());
        assertEquals(3, o.timeout().toMinutes());
    }

    @Test void atModeNeedsNoRange() throws Exception {
        Cli.Options o = parse("appC", "--at", "HEAD");
        assertEquals("HEAD", o.at());
        assertNull(o.from());
    }

    @Test void repeatedBooleanFlagsAreHarmless() throws Exception {
        assertFalse(parse("appA", "--from", "x", "--to", "y", "--no-bazel", "--no-bazel").bazel());
    }

    @Test void rejectsMissingApp() {
        UsageException e = fails();
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("missing <app>"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsFlagInAppPosition() {
        UsageException e = fails("--from", "x", "--to", "y");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--from"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsMissingTo() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--from", "x");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--to"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsAtTogetherWithRange() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--from", "x", "--to", "y", "--at", "z");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--at"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsUnknownFlag() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--bogus");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--bogus"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsNonNumericTimeout() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--timeout-minutes", "soon");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes needs a whole number, got 'soon'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsFlagWithoutValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--at needs a value"), e.getMessage());
    }

    // A value-taking option missing its value at the end of the arguments is a usage error naming that
    // option, same as --at above.
    @Test void rejectsFromWithoutValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--from");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--from needs a value"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsToWithoutValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--to");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--to needs a value"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsTaskWithoutValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--task");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--task needs a value"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsTsvOutWithoutValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--tsv-out");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--tsv-out needs a value"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsTimeoutMinutesWithoutValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--timeout-minutes");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes needs a value"), e.getMessage());
    }

    // DefaultProcessRunner throws an unchecked exception for a non-positive timeout, so the CLI must
    // reject it as a usage error before it ever reaches the runner.
    @Test void rejectsZeroTimeout() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--timeout-minutes", "0");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes must be a positive integer, got 0"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsNegativeTimeout() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--timeout-minutes", "-5");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes must be a positive integer, got -5"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsTimeoutThatOverflowsDuration() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--timeout-minutes", String.valueOf(Long.MAX_VALUE));
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes is too large, got '" + Long.MAX_VALUE + "'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsTimeoutWithTooManyDigitsToFitALong() {
        String huge = "9".repeat(20);
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--timeout-minutes", huge);
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes is too large, got '" + huge + "'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsTimeoutWithNonAsciiDigit() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--timeout-minutes", "٥");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes needs a whole number, got '٥'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    // --timeout-minutes is exempt from the generic "looks like a flag" check (unlike --from, --to, --at,
    // --task and --tsv-out above), so its value always reaches parseMinutes, which reports a value that
    // merely fails to parse as "needs a whole number", naming the option, rather than "needs a value".
    @Test void rejectsTimeoutValueThatLooksLikeAFlag() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--timeout-minutes", "--offline");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes needs a whole number, got '--offline'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsBlankTimeoutValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--timeout-minutes", "");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes needs a whole number, got ''"), e.getMessage());
    }

    // A negative value with too many digits to fit a long overflows Long.parseLong before the sign is
    // ever inspected, so it is reported as "too large" rather than "must be a positive integer".
    @Test void rejectsNegativeTimeoutWithTooManyDigitsToFitALong() {
        String huge = "-" + "9".repeat(20);
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--timeout-minutes", huge);
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--timeout-minutes is too large, got '" + huge + "'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void usageMentionsEveryFlag() {
        for (String flag : new String[] {"--from", "--to", "--at", "--task", "--no-bazel", "--offline", "--keep-worktrees", "--tsv", "--tsv-out", "--timeout-minutes"}) {
            assertTrue(Cli.USAGE.contains(flag), flag);
        }
    }

    // The JVM ignores $TMPDIR on Linux (java.io.tmpdir is always /tmp), so the default scratch root
    // reads it directly to keep the documented $TMPDIR/gradle-tracer behaviour true.
    @Test void defaultScratchRootPrefersTmpdirEnvWhenSet() {
        Path root = Cli.defaultScratchRoot(name -> "TMPDIR".equals(name) ? "/custom/tmp" : null, () -> "/fallback");
        assertEquals(Path.of("/custom/tmp", "gradle-tracer"), root);
    }

    @Test void defaultScratchRootFallsBackToJavaIoTmpdirWhenTmpdirUnset() {
        Path root = Cli.defaultScratchRoot(name -> null, () -> "/fallback");
        assertEquals(Path.of("/fallback", "gradle-tracer"), root);
    }

    @Test void defaultScratchRootFallsBackToJavaIoTmpdirWhenTmpdirBlank() {
        Path root = Cli.defaultScratchRoot(name -> "", () -> "/fallback");
        assertEquals(Path.of("/fallback", "gradle-tracer"), root);
    }

    // Child processes such as <worktree>/gradlew run inside the worktree, so a relative root would
    // resolve differently there; resolve it once against the JVM's working directory (the repo root
    // after ./trace's cd).
    @Test void defaultScratchRootIsAbsoluteEvenForARelativeTmpdir() {
        Path root = Cli.defaultScratchRoot(name -> "TMPDIR".equals(name) ? "rel/dir" : null, () -> "/fallback");
        assertTrue(root.isAbsolute(), root.toString());
        assertEquals(Path.of("rel/dir", "gradle-tracer").toAbsolutePath().normalize(), root);
    }

    // Every value-taking option rejects a value that is blank or looks like another flag, instead of
    // silently swallowing the next option (e.g. "--from --to y" would otherwise parse "--to" as the
    // value of --from and then fail on "y" as an unknown option).
    @Test void rejectsFromValueThatLooksLikeAFlag() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--from", "--to", "y");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--from needs a value, got '--to'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsBlankFromValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--from", "", "--to", "y");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--from needs a value, got ''"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsToValueThatLooksLikeAFlag() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--from", "x", "--to", "--task");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--to needs a value, got '--task'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsBlankToValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--from", "x", "--to", "");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--to needs a value, got ''"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsAtValueThatLooksLikeAFlag() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "--offline");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--at needs a value, got '--offline'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsBlankAtValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--at needs a value, got ''"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsTaskValueThatLooksLikeAFlag() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--task", "--offline");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--task needs a value, got '--offline'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsBlankTaskValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--task", "");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--task needs a value, got ''"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsTsvOutValueThatLooksLikeAFlag() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--from", "x", "--to", "y", "--tsv-out", "--timeout-minutes");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--tsv-out needs a value, got '--timeout-minutes'"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsBlankTsvOutValue() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--from", "x", "--to", "y", "--tsv-out", "");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--tsv-out needs a value, got ''"), e.getMessage());
    }

    // <app> must match [A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*; path-like or option-like names are rejected outright
    // instead of reaching the filesystem as a directory lookup.
    @Test void rejectsBlankAppName() {
        UsageException e = fails("");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("app name"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsDashHAsAppName() {
        UsageException e = fails("-h");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("-h"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsDotAsAppName() {
        UsageException e = fails(".");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("."), e.getMessage());
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("is not a valid app name"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsDotDotAsAppName() {
        UsageException e = fails("..");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains(".."), e.getMessage());
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("is not a valid app name"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void rejectsPathTraversalAppName() {
        UsageException e = fails("../..");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("../.."), e.getMessage());
    }

    // --at (spec: "print the key and its composition, no comparison") makes --tsv, --tsv-out and
    // --no-bazel meaningless; each is rejected rather than silently ignored.
    @Test void atRejectsTsv() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--tsv");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--at") && e.getMessage().contains("--tsv"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void atRejectsTsvOut() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--tsv-out", "rows.tsv");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--at") && e.getMessage().contains("--tsv-out"), e.getMessage());
    }

    @Test void atRejectsNoBazel() {
        UsageException e = fails("appA", "--at", "z", "--no-bazel");
        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("--at") && e.getMessage().contains("--no-bazel"), e.getMessage());
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Replace `tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java` with:
```java
package io.joeyang.tracer.cli;

import java.io.PrintStream;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.function.Function;
import java.util.function.Supplier;
import java.util.regex.Pattern;

/** Entry point. Options parsing lives here; the range walk arrives in the next task. */
public final class Cli {
    public static final int EXIT_OK = 0;
    public static final int EXIT_USAGE = 1;
    public static final int EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE = 2;
    public static final int EXIT_CHANGED = 3;

    // The JVM ignores $TMPDIR on Linux (java.io.tmpdir is always /tmp), so read it directly to keep the
    // documented $TMPDIR/gradle-tracer behaviour true. Wired into main() in the next task.
    static final Path DEFAULT_SCRATCH = defaultScratchRoot(System::getenv, () -> System.getProperty("java.io.tmpdir"));

    // <app> is used as a directory name under app/; restrict it so a path-like or option-like value
    // never reaches the filesystem lookup.
    private static final Pattern APP_NAME = Pattern.compile("[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*");

    static final String USAGE = """
            usage: trace <app> --from <rev> --to <rev> [--task <name>] [--no-bazel] [--offline] [--keep-worktrees]
                                                        [--tsv] [--tsv-out <file>] [--timeout-minutes <n>]
                   trace <app> --at <rev> [--task <name>] [--offline] [--keep-worktrees] [--timeout-minutes <n>]

              <app>            appA | appB | appC   (maps to :app:<app>:<task> and //app/<app>:<app>)
              --task           Gradle task whose cache key is compared; default compileJava, alternative fingerprint
              --no-bazel       skip the bazel-diff column
              --offline        pass --offline to gradle; forbid repository downloads in bazel
              --keep-worktrees leave the checked-out worktrees and hash files in place for inspection
              --tsv            machine-readable output only (sha, subject, key, bazel, verdict, culprits)
              --tsv-out <file> print the table and also write the TSV to <file>
              --timeout-minutes per external command, default 10

            exit codes: 0 no change, 1 usage error, 2 a tool failed, 3 at least one CHANGED
            """;

    /** Parsed command line. */
    record Options(String app, String from, String to, String at, String task, boolean bazel, boolean offline,
                   boolean keepWorktrees, boolean tsv, Path tsvOut, Duration timeout) {

        static Options parse(String[] args) throws UsageException {
            if (args.length == 0) {
                throw new UsageException("missing <app>");
            }
            String app = args[0];
            if (!APP_NAME.matcher(app).matches()) {
                throw new UsageException("'" + app + "' is not a valid app name: expected letters, digits, "
                        + "'-' or '_', starting with a letter or digit");
            }
            String from = null, to = null, at = null, task = "compileJava";
            boolean bazel = true, offline = false, keep = false, tsv = false;
            Path tsvOut = null;
            Duration timeout = Duration.ofMinutes(10);
            for (int i = 1; i < args.length; i++) {
                String flag = args[i];
                switch (flag) {
                    case "--no-bazel" -> bazel = false;
                    case "--offline" -> offline = true;
                    case "--keep-worktrees" -> keep = true;
                    case "--tsv" -> tsv = true;
                    case "--from", "--to", "--at", "--task", "--tsv-out", "--timeout-minutes" -> {
                        if (i + 1 >= args.length) {
                            throw new UsageException(flag + " needs a value");
                        }
                        String value = args[++i];
                        // A blank value, or one that looks like another flag, is almost always a missing
                        // value swallowing the next option, e.g. "--from --to y" would otherwise parse
                        // "--to" as --from's value and then fail on "y" as unknown. Excluded for
                        // --timeout-minutes: its own numeric validation below gives a strictly more
                        // specific error for a negative or zero value than this generic message would.
                        if (!flag.equals("--timeout-minutes") && (value.isBlank() || value.startsWith("-"))) {
                            throw new UsageException(flag + " needs a value, got '" + value + "'");
                        }
                        switch (flag) {
                            case "--from" -> from = value;
                            case "--to" -> to = value;
                            case "--at" -> at = value;
                            case "--task" -> task = value;
                            case "--tsv-out" -> tsvOut = Path.of(value);
                            default -> timeout = parseMinutes(value);
                        }
                    }
                    default -> throw new UsageException("unknown option '" + flag + "'");
                }
            }
            if (at != null && (from != null || to != null)) {
                throw new UsageException("use either --at or --from/--to, not both");
            }
            if (at == null && (from == null || to == null)) {
                throw new UsageException("--from and --to are both required (or use --at)");
            }
            // --at prints the key and its composition with no comparison (spec §5), so the
            // comparison-only flags are meaningless alongside it.
            if (at != null && tsv) {
                throw new UsageException("--at cannot be combined with --tsv");
            }
            if (at != null && tsvOut != null) {
                throw new UsageException("--at cannot be combined with --tsv-out");
            }
            if (at != null && !bazel) {
                throw new UsageException("--at cannot be combined with --no-bazel");
            }
            return new Options(app, from, to, at, task, bazel, offline, keep, tsv, tsvOut, timeout);
        }

        // ASCII digits only (with an optional leading '-'), checked before parsing, so a non-ASCII digit
        // (e.g. the Arabic-indic '5') is reported as "needs a whole number", and a value that IS
        // all-ASCII-digits but too long to fit in a long is distinguished as "too large" instead.
        private static final Pattern TIMEOUT_VALUE = Pattern.compile("-?[0-9]+");

        private static Duration parseMinutes(String value) throws UsageException {
            if (!TIMEOUT_VALUE.matcher(value).matches()) {
                throw new UsageException("--timeout-minutes needs a whole number, got '" + value + "'");
            }
            long minutes;
            try {
                minutes = Long.parseLong(value);
            } catch (NumberFormatException e) {
                // A value with too many digits to fit in a long overflows here, including a negative one
                // (e.g. a minus sign followed by twenty 9s): "too large" fits either sign equally well.
                throw new UsageException("--timeout-minutes is too large, got '" + value + "'");
            }
            // DefaultProcessRunner rejects a zero or negative timeout with an unchecked exception, so
            // validate it here as a usage error instead of ever passing one through.
            if (minutes <= 0) {
                throw new UsageException("--timeout-minutes must be a positive integer, got " + minutes);
            }
            try {
                return Duration.ofMinutes(minutes);
            } catch (ArithmeticException e) {
                // Duration.ofMinutes overflows for absurdly large inputs; map that to a usage error instead
                // of letting ArithmeticException escape main().
                throw new UsageException("--timeout-minutes is too large, got '" + value + "'");
            }
        }

        String taskPath() {
            return ":app:" + app + ":" + task;
        }

        String bazelTarget() {
            return "//app/" + app + ":" + app;
        }
    }

    private Cli() {}

    // Package-private and parameterized by lookup functions so the TMPDIR-vs-fallback behaviour is
    // directly testable without touching real environment variables or system properties.
    static Path defaultScratchRoot(Function<String, String> getenv, Supplier<String> javaIoTmpdir) {
        String tmpdir = getenv.apply("TMPDIR");
        String base = (tmpdir != null && !tmpdir.isBlank()) ? tmpdir : javaIoTmpdir.get();
        // Child processes such as <worktree>/gradlew run inside the worktree, so a relative root would
        // resolve differently there; resolve it once against the JVM's working directory (the repo root
        // after ./trace's cd).
        return Path.of(base, "gradle-tracer").toAbsolutePath().normalize();
    }

    static int usage(PrintStream err, String problem) {
        err.println("trace: " + problem);
        err.print(USAGE);
        return EXIT_USAGE;
    }

    public static void main(String[] args) {
        try {
            Options.parse(args);
        } catch (UsageException e) {
            System.exit(usage(System.err, e.getMessage()));
            return;
        }
        System.err.println("trace: options accepted; the range walk arrives in a later commit");
        System.exit(EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE);
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Document and commit**

Update `docs/tracer.md`: the intro paragraph now says "Today `Cli.main` parses and validates options. On a
usage error it exits 1 with the usage text. Valid options currently exit 2, printing a not-implemented message,
because no verdict is produced yet. The range walk, the `./trace` wrapper and exit codes 0 and 3 arrive in later
commits."; the `UsageException`/`ProviderException` vocabulary row and the exit-codes line both note which codes
are wired today versus by a later commit; and the `## Cli` section is:
```markdown
## Cli
`trace <app> --from A --to B [--task T] [--no-bazel] [--offline] [--keep-worktrees] [--tsv] [--tsv-out F] [--timeout-minutes N]`
and `trace <app> --at REV [--task T] [--offline] [--keep-worktrees] [--timeout-minutes N]`.

`<app>` must match `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*`; a blank, path-like (`.`, `..`, `../..`) or option-like (`-h`) name
is a usage error naming the rejected value. Every value-taking option (`--from`, `--to`, `--at`, `--task`,
`--tsv-out`) rejects a value that is blank or starts with `-`, so a missing value can't silently swallow the
next option: `--from --to y` fails naming `--from`, rather than failing later on `y` as an unknown option; a
value missing at the end of the arguments fails the same way, naming the option. The one exception is
`--timeout-minutes`: its values are validated as integers instead (see below). `--at` prints the key and its
composition with no comparison, so it rejects `--tsv`, `--tsv-out` and `--no-bazel` alongside it, naming both
flags in the error.

`Cli.defaultScratchRoot` computes one fixed scratch root, `$TMPDIR/gradle-tracer`, that worktrees and bazel-diff
hash files will live under once the range walk is wired (never per pid — a fixed root is how the next run finds
and sweeps a killed run's leftovers). On Linux the JVM does not read `$TMPDIR` into `java.io.tmpdir` (it is
always `/tmp`), so it reads the `TMPDIR` environment variable directly and only falls back to `java.io.tmpdir`
when it is unset or blank, keeping the documented path real; either way, the result is resolved to an absolute,
normalized path, since a child process such as `<worktree>/gradlew` runs inside the worktree, so a relative
root would resolve differently there — it is resolved once against the JVM's working directory (the repo root
after `./trace`'s `cd`) instead.

`--timeout-minutes` values are validated as integers instead of the generic blank-or-dash check above. The
value is checked against ASCII digits with an optional leading `-` before parsing: anything else, including a
non-ASCII digit, is "needs a whole number"; a value that is all ASCII digits but too large to fit a `long`
(including a negative one that is too long to fit), or that overflows `Duration.ofMinutes`, is "too large"; and
a value that parses but is zero or negative is "must be a positive integer". `DefaultProcessRunner` rejects a
non-positive timeout with an unchecked exception, so the CLI validates it here as a usage error instead of ever
passing one through.
```

```bash
git add tools/tracer docs/tracer.md
git commit -m "feat(tracer): parse command-line options

Options are an immutable record parsed before anything runs, so every
mistake on the command line is a usage error with exit code 1 before a
worktree is created. Kept apart from the range walk to stay under the
commit size limit.

Two amendments from code review: --timeout-minutes now rejects zero,
negative and overflowing values as usage errors, since
DefaultProcessRunner throws unchecked exceptions for a non-positive
timeout and must never receive one; and the default scratch root
reads $TMPDIR from the environment directly, since the JVM ignores it
in java.io.tmpdir on Linux, falling back to java.io.tmpdir when unset
or blank."
```

A second code-review round on this commit found four more gaps, fixed in a follow-up commit: (1) every
value-taking option silently swallowed the next flag as its own value when one was missing (`--from --to y`
parsed `--to` as the value of `--from` and then failed on `y` as an unknown option) — a blank or flag-shaped
value is now rejected by name, except for `--timeout-minutes`, which keeps its own more specific numeric
message; (2) `<app>` was validated only against a loose `--` prefix check, so `.`, `..`, `../..`, `-h` and a
blank name all reached the filesystem lookup — it must now match `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*`; (3) `--at` (spec:
"print the key and its composition, no comparison") silently accepted `--tsv`, `--tsv-out` and `--no-bazel`,
which make no sense without a comparison — each is now rejected by name alongside `--at`; (4)
`defaultScratchRoot` could return a relative path for a relative `TMPDIR` — a child process such as
`<worktree>/gradlew` runs inside the worktree, so a relative root would resolve differently there, and it now
returns an absolute, normalized path, resolved once against the JVM's working directory instead. A third
round added missing-value tests for the remaining options (only `--at` had one before), tightened
`docs/tracer.md` to describe the scratch root as something `defaultScratchRoot` computes rather than something
already live, added the reason for the interim exit code to the intro paragraph, and rewrote every
review-history code comment ("Amendment (code review): ...", "Fix (code review round 2): ...") to state the
reason for the code instead of the fact that review produced it. A fourth, small follow-up corrected the
scratch-root rationale again — the earlier wording blamed `./trace`'s directory change, when the real reason is
that a child process's working directory (the worktree, not the repo root) is what a relative path would
otherwise resolve against — dropped `--timeout-minutes` from the list of options covered by the generic
blank-or-dash check (it was already carved out as the one exception, so listing it there too was redundant),
noted that an overlong negative value is also "too large", and strengthened the `.`/`..` app-name assertions to
check the message names the pattern violation, not just that it contains the literal characters.

Provider constructors used here and implemented in Tasks 7 and 8 — the signatures are fixed:
`new GradleKeyProvider(ProcessRunner runner, boolean offline, Duration timeout)` and
`new BazelDiffProvider(ProcessRunner runner, Path repo, boolean offline, Duration timeout)`.

Scratch hygiene (spec §9): one fixed scratch root (`$TMPDIR/gradle-tracer`), never per pid, so the next run finds a killed run's leftovers. `WorktreePool` sweeps stale `wt-*` directories (Task 9); the Cli sweeps stale `hashes-*.json`/`*.impacted.txt` at start and removes the ones it created at the end unless `--keep-worktrees`.

This task lands as seven commits instead of one, because the full wiring exceeds the 400-line hard commit limit.
Every diff below is the exact diff that was committed (`git diff <parent> <sha> -- <path>`), so it can be checked
mechanically against the real history rather than re-typed by hand. Every commit block below uses
`git commit -F - <<'EOF' ... EOF` rather than `git commit -m "..."`, so a backticked word inside the message (e.g.
`` `trace` ``) can't be read as a shell command substitution if the block is copied and pasted as-is.

---

#### Commit A — `feat(tracer): walk the commit range from the CLI`

Wires the constructor, `run`, `range` and `deleteScratchFiles` (the stale sweep at start and the cleanup at
end), and the real `main`. `--at` is still a placeholder here (prints "--at arrives in a later commit" to
stderr, exits `EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE`) because `RevisionRange.resolveOne`, added in this same commit, isn't wired
into `Cli` until Commit B.

Also adds, beyond the original single-commit plan: a pre-check of `--tsv-out` (the parent directory must exist,
be a directory, and be writable, and the target must not already be a directory) done before any worktree is
created or any git command beyond option parsing runs; and a test that an unknown `--from` revision exits 1.

```diff
+    }
+
     private String revParse(String rev) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
         ProcessRunner.Result r = run("git", "rev-parse", "--verify", "--quiet", rev + "^{commit}");
         // Under --quiet, real git exits 1 (with no stderr) for a revision it simply cannot resolve. Any other
```

```diff
-/** Entry point. Options parsing lives here; the range walk arrives in the next task. */
+/** Entry point: parses options and walks the range through worktrees, printing the verdicts. {@code --at} is a placeholder until a later commit. */

+    /** One fixed scratch root, so the next run finds and removes whatever a killed run left behind. */
     static final Path DEFAULT_SCRATCH = defaultScratchRoot(System::getenv, () -> System.getProperty("java.io.tmpdir"));

@@ -161,20 +180,155 @@ public final class Cli {

+    public static void main(String[] args) {
+        Options options;
+        try {
+            options = Options.parse(args);
+        } catch (UsageException e) {
+            System.exit(usage(System.err, e.getMessage()));
+            return;
+        }
+        ProcessRunner runner = new DefaultProcessRunner();
+        Path repo = Path.of("").toAbsolutePath();
+        KeyProvider keys = new GradleKeyProvider(runner, options.offline(), options.timeout());
+        ImpactProvider impacts = options.bazel() ? new BazelDiffProvider(runner, repo, options.offline(), options.timeout()) : null;
+        System.exit(new Cli(runner, repo, DEFAULT_SCRATCH, keys, impacts).run(options, System.out, System.err));

     static int usage(PrintStream err, String problem) {
         err.println("trace: " + problem);
         err.print(USAGE);
         return EXIT_USAGE;
     }
 
-    public static void main(String[] args) {
+    int run(Options o, PrintStream out, PrintStream err) {
         try {
-            Options.parse(args);
+            if (!Files.isDirectory(repo.resolve("app").resolve(o.app()))) {
+                throw new UsageException("unknown app '" + o.app() + "': expected a directory app/" + o.app());
+            }
+            if (o.at() != null) {
+                err.println("trace: --at arrives in a later commit");
+                return EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE;
+            }
+            checkTsvOutWritable(o);
+            return range(o, out, err);
         } catch (UsageException e) {
-            System.exit(usage(System.err, e.getMessage()));
+            return usage(err, e.getMessage());
+        } catch (ProviderException e) {
+            err.println("trace: " + e.getMessage());
+            return EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE;
+        }
+    }
+
+    /**
+     * Checked before any worktree is created or any git command beyond option parsing runs, so a bad
+     * {@code --tsv-out} path fails fast instead of after a whole range walk has already run.
+     */
+    private void checkTsvOutWritable(Options o) throws UsageException {
+        Path tsvOut = o.tsvOut();
+        if (tsvOut == null) {
             return;
         }
-        System.err.println("trace: options accepted; the range walk arrives in a later commit");
-        System.exit(EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE);
+        if (Files.isDirectory(tsvOut)) {
+            throw new UsageException("--tsv-out '" + tsvOut + "' is a directory");
+        }
+        Path parent = tsvOut.toAbsolutePath().getParent();
+        if (parent == null) {
+            parent = Path.of("").toAbsolutePath();
+        }
+        if (!Files.isDirectory(parent)) {
+            throw new UsageException("--tsv-out '" + tsvOut + "': parent directory " + parent + " does not exist");
+        }
+        if (!Files.isWritable(parent)) {
+            throw new UsageException("--tsv-out '" + tsvOut + "': parent directory " + parent + " is not writable");
+        }
+    }
+
+    private int range(Options o, PrintStream out, PrintStream err) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
+        List<Commit> commits = new RevisionRange(runner, repo).resolve(o.from(), o.to());
+        int swept = deleteScratchFiles();
+        if (swept > 0) {
+            err.println("trace: removed " + swept + " stale scratch file(s) under " + scratch);
+        }
+        List<Verdict> rows = new ArrayList<>();
+        try (WorktreePool pool = new WorktreePool(runner, repo, scratch, o.keepWorktrees())) {
+            Optional<TaskKey> previousGood = Optional.empty();
+            Path previousHashes = null;
+            boolean bazelAlive = impacts != null;
+            for (Commit commit : commits) {
+                Path worktree = pool.checkout(commit);
+                Optional<TaskKey> key = Optional.empty();
+                Optional<String> error = Optional.empty();
+                try {
+                    key = Optional.of(keys.keyFor(worktree, o.taskPath()));
+                } catch (ProviderException e) {
+                    error = Optional.of(e.getMessage());
+                }
+                BazelStatus bazel = BazelStatus.NONE;
+                if (bazelAlive) {
+                    try {
+                        Path hashes = impacts.hashes(worktree, scratch.resolve("hashes-" + commit.shortSha() + ".json"));
+                        if (previousHashes != null) {
+                            Set<String> impacted = impacts.impactedTargets(previousHashes, hashes);
+                            bazel = impacted.contains(o.bazelTarget()) ? BazelStatus.IMPACTED : BazelStatus.CLEAN;
+                        }
+                        previousHashes = hashes;
+                    } catch (ProviderException e) {
+                        err.println("trace: bazel-diff unavailable: " + e.getMessage());
+                        bazelAlive = false;
+                        bazel = BazelStatus.NOT_AVAILABLE;
+                    }
+                } else if (impacts != null) {
+                    bazel = BazelStatus.NOT_AVAILABLE;
+                }
+                rows.add(KeyComparator.verdict(commit, previousGood, key, error, bazel));
+                if (key.isPresent()) {
+                    previousGood = key;
+                }
+                if (!o.keepWorktrees()) {
+                    pool.release(worktree);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if (!o.keepWorktrees()) {
+            deleteScratchFiles();
+        }
+        String tsv = TableRenderer.renderTsv(rows);
+        if (o.tsv()) {
+            out.print(tsv);
+        } else {
+            out.print(TableRenderer.render(o.taskPath(), impacts == null ? null : o.bazelTarget(), rows));
+        }
+        if (o.tsvOut() != null) {
+            try {
+                Files.writeString(o.tsvOut(), tsv);
+            } catch (IOException e) {
+                throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + o.tsvOut() + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
+            }
+        }
+        if (rows.stream().anyMatch(v -> v.status() == Status.BUILD_FAILED)) {
+            return EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE;
+        }
+        return rows.stream().anyMatch(v -> v.status() == Status.CHANGED) ? EXIT_CHANGED : EXIT_OK;
+    }
+
+    /** Deletes bazel-diff hash and impacted-target files under the scratch root; returns how many went. */
+    private int deleteScratchFiles() {
+        if (!Files.isDirectory(scratch)) {
+            return 0;
+        }
+        int count = 0;
+        try (Stream<Path> entries = Files.list(scratch)) {
+            for (Path p : entries.toList()) {
+                String name = p.getFileName().toString();
+                if (Files.isRegularFile(p) && (name.startsWith("hashes-") || name.endsWith(".impacted.txt"))) {
+                    Files.deleteIfExists(p);
+                    count++;
+                }
+            }
+        } catch (IOException ignored) {
+            // best effort
+        }
+        return count;
     }
 }
```

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliTest.java b/tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliTest.java
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..15fbada
--- /dev/null
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliTest.java
@@ -0,0 +1,177 @@
+package io.joeyang.tracer.cli;
+
+import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertEquals;
+import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertFalse;
+import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertTrue;
+
+import java.io.ByteArrayOutputStream;
+import java.io.IOException;
+import java.io.PrintStream;
+import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
+import java.nio.file.Files;
+import java.nio.file.Path;
+import java.util.Map;
+import java.util.Set;
+import java.util.stream.Stream;
+import org.junit.jupiter.api.BeforeEach;
+import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test;
+import org.junit.jupiter.api.io.TempDir;
+
+class CliTest {
+    static final String A = "a".repeat(40), B = "b".repeat(40), C = "c".repeat(40);
+    @TempDir Path tmp;
+    Path repo, scratch;
+    FakeProcessRunner git;
+    final ByteArrayOutputStream out = new ByteArrayOutputStream();
+    final ByteArrayOutputStream err = new ByteArrayOutputStream();
+
+    @BeforeEach
+    void setUp() throws IOException {
+        repo = tmp.resolve("repo");
+        scratch = tmp.resolve("scratch");
+        Files.createDirectories(repo.resolve("app/appA"));
+        git = new FakeProcessRunner()
+                .on("git rev-parse --verify --quiet base^{commit}", 0, A + "\n", "")
+                .on("git rev-parse --verify --quiet tip^{commit}", 0, C + "\n", "")
+                .on("git log -1 --format=%H%x1f%P%x1f%s " + A, 0, A + "\u001f\u001fbaseline\n", "")
+                .on("git log -1 --format=%H%x1f%P%x1f%s " + C, 0, C + "\u001f" + B + "\u001fdocs only\n", "")
+                .on("git log --reverse --first-parent --format=%H%x1f%P%x1f%s " + A + ".." + C, 0,
+                        B + "\u001f" + A + "\u001fcore: add shout\n" + C + "\u001f" + B + "\u001fdocs only\n", "")
+                .on("git worktree", 0, "", "");
+    }
+
+    static TaskKey key(String task, String hash, String jarHash) {
+        return new TaskKey(task, hash, Map.of("classpath", Map.of("/wt/core/build/libs/core.jar", jarHash)), Map.of(), "impl");
+    }
+
+    final KeyProvider keys = (wt, task) -> switch (wt.getFileName().toString()) {
+        case "wt-aaaaaaa" -> key(task, "k1", "h1");
+        case "wt-bbbbbbb", "wt-ccccccc" -> key(task, "k2", "h2");
+        default -> throw new ProviderException("unexpected worktree " + wt);
+    };
+
+    /** Writes a hashes file like the real tool does, and reports appA impacted for commit B only. */
+    final ImpactProvider impacts = new ImpactProvider() {
+        @Override public Path hashes(Path worktree, Path output) throws ProviderException {
+            try {
+                return Files.writeString(output, "{}");
+            } catch (IOException e) {
+                throw new ProviderException(e.getMessage(), e);
+            }
+        }
+        @Override public Set<String> impactedTargets(Path previous, Path current) {
+            return current.getFileName().toString().contains("bbbbbbb") ? Set.of("//app/appA:appA") : Set.of();
+        }
+    };
+
+    int run(KeyProvider k, ImpactProvider i, String... args) throws UsageException {
+        return new Cli(git, repo, scratch, k, i).run(Cli.Options.parse(args),
+                new PrintStream(out, true, StandardCharsets.UTF_8), new PrintStream(err, true, StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
+    }
+
+    String out() { return out.toString(StandardCharsets.UTF_8); }
+    String err() { return err.toString(StandardCharsets.UTF_8); }
+
+    @Test void rangeRendersTableAndExits3WhenSomethingChanged() throws Exception {
+        int code = run(keys, impacts, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip");
+
+        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_CHANGED, code, err());
+        assertTrue(out().contains("bbbbbbb  core: add shout") && out().contains("CHANGED") && out().contains("core.jar"), out());
+        assertTrue(out().contains("ccccccc  docs only") && out().contains("clean"), out());
+        assertTrue(out().contains("//app/appA:appA (bazel-diff)"), out());
+        assertTrue(git.ran("git worktree remove --force"), "worktrees must be removed");
+    }
+
+    @Test void tsvGoesToStdoutWithoutTable() throws Exception {
+        run(keys, impacts, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip", "--tsv");
+        assertTrue(out().startsWith(A + "\tbaseline\tk1\t-\tbase\t"), out());
+        assertEquals(3, out().split("\n").length);
+    }
+
+    @Test void tsvOutWritesFileAndKeepsTheTable() throws Exception {
+        Path file = tmp.resolve("rows.tsv");
+        run(keys, impacts, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip", "--tsv-out", file.toString());
+        assertTrue(out().contains("Tracing inputs of"), out());
+        assertTrue(Files.readString(file).contains(B + "\tcore: add shout\tk2\timpacted\tCHANGED\tcore.jar"));
+    }
+
+    @Test void keyFailureForOneCommitGivesBuildFailedRowAndExit2() throws Exception {
+        KeyProvider flaky = (wt, task) -> {
+            if (wt.getFileName().toString().equals("wt-bbbbbbb")) {
+                throw new ProviderException("Could not resolve com.example:missing:1.0");
+            }
+            return keys.keyFor(wt, task);
+        };
+        int code = run(flaky, impacts, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip", "--tsv");
+
+        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE, code);
+        String[] rows = out().split("\n");
+        assertTrue(rows[1].contains("\tBUILD-FAILED\tCould not resolve com.example:missing:1.0"), rows[1]);
+        assertTrue(rows[2].contains("\tCHANGED\t"), "third commit is compared with the last good key: " + rows[2]);
+    }
+
+    @Test void bazelFailureDegradesToNotAvailable() throws Exception {
+        ImpactProvider broken = new ImpactProvider() {
+            @Override public Path hashes(Path worktree, Path output) throws ProviderException { throw new ProviderException("bazel: command not found"); }
+            @Override public Set<String> impactedTargets(Path p, Path c) { return Set.of(); }
+        };
+        int code = run(keys, broken, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip", "--tsv");
+
+        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_CHANGED, code);
+        assertTrue(err().contains("bazel-diff unavailable: bazel: command not found"), err());
+        assertTrue(out().split("\n")[1].contains("\tn/a\t"), out());
+    }
+
+    @Test void noBazelOmitsTheColumnSource() throws Exception {
+        run(keys, null, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip", "--no-bazel");
+        assertFalse(out().contains("bazel-diff)"), out());
+        assertTrue(out().contains("Tracing inputs of :app:appA:compileJava (gradle) over 2 commits"), out());
+    }
+
+    @Test void unknownAppIsAUsageError() throws Exception {
+        int code = run(keys, impacts, "appZ", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip");
+        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_USAGE, code);
+        assertTrue(err().contains("appZ"), err());
+    }
+
+    @Test void unknownFromRevisionIsAUsageError() throws Exception {
+        git.on("git rev-parse --verify --quiet nope^{commit}", 1, "", "");
+        int code = run(keys, impacts, "appA", "--from", "nope", "--to", "tip");
+        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_USAGE, code, err());
+        assertTrue(err().contains("nope"), err());
+    }
+
+    @Test void atArrivesInALaterCommit() throws Exception {
+        int code = run(keys, impacts, "appA", "--at", "tip", "--task", "fingerprint");
+        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE, code);
+        assertTrue(err().contains("--at arrives in a later commit"), err());
+    }
+
+    @Test void tsvOutWithMissingParentIsAUsageError() throws Exception {
+        Path file = tmp.resolve("missing-dir/rows.tsv");
+        int code = run(keys, impacts, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip", "--tsv-out", file.toString());
+        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_USAGE, code, err());
+        assertTrue(err().contains(file.toString()), err());
+        assertFalse(git.ran("git worktree"), "no worktree work before the tsv-out pre-check fails");
+    }
+
+    @Test void tsvOutThatIsADirectoryIsAUsageError() throws Exception {
+        int code = run(keys, impacts, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip", "--tsv-out", tmp.toString());
+        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_USAGE, code, err());
+        assertTrue(err().contains(tmp.toString()), err());
+        assertFalse(git.ran("git worktree"), "no worktree work before the tsv-out pre-check fails");
+    }
+
+    @Test void staleScratchFilesAreSweptAndNewOnesRemoved() throws Exception {
+        Files.createDirectories(scratch);
+        Path stale = Files.writeString(scratch.resolve("hashes-stale.json"), "{}");
+
+        run(keys, impacts, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip", "--tsv");
+
+        assertFalse(Files.exists(stale), "a killed run's hash files are removed at start");
+        assertTrue(err().contains("removed 1 stale scratch file"), err());
+        try (Stream<Path> left = Files.list(scratch)) {
+            assertTrue(left.noneMatch(p -> p.getFileName().toString().startsWith("hashes-")), "hash files created by this run are removed at the end");
+        }
+    }
+}
```

```diff
diff --git a/docs/tracer.md b/docs/tracer.md
index 93473b4..49a8726 100644
--- a/docs/tracer.md
+++ b/docs/tracer.md
@@ -1,8 +1,9 @@
 # tracer
 
-`tools/tracer` is a Java CLI. Today `Cli.main` parses and validates options. On a usage error it exits 1 with the
-usage text. Valid options currently exit 2, printing a not-implemented message, because no verdict is produced
-yet. The range walk, the `./trace` wrapper and exit codes 0 and 3 arrive in later commits.
+`tools/tracer` is a Java CLI. `Cli.main` parses options, then walks the `--from`/`--to` range through detached
+git worktrees, asking the Gradle and bazel-diff providers about each commit and printing a verdict table. On a
+usage error it exits 1 with the usage text. `--at` currently exits 2 with a placeholder message; it and the
+`./trace` wrapper arrive in later commits.
 
 ## Vocabulary (`io.joeyang.tracer.cli`)
 | type | meaning |
@@ -15,12 +16,11 @@ yet. The range walk, the `./trace` wrapper and exit codes 0 and 3 arrive in late
 | `BazelStatus` | `IMPACTED`, `CLEAN`, `NOT_AVAILABLE`, `NONE` — the informational column |
 | `Verdict` | one table row |
 | `KeyProvider`, `ImpactProvider` | the two oracles behind interfaces so tests can fake them |
-| `UsageException` → exit 1 (wired); `ProviderException` → exit 2, wired by a later commit | error classes |
+| `UsageException` → exit 1; `ProviderException` → exit 2 | error classes |
 
 ## Exit codes
-0 no change in range · 1 usage error (wired) · 2 a tool failed (some row is BUILD-FAILED) · 3 at least one
-CHANGED — 0, the "a tool failed" sense of 2, and 3 are wired by a later commit; `Cli.main` exits 2 today as an
-interim placeholder whenever the options are valid.
+0 no change in range · 1 usage error · 2 a tool failed (some row is BUILD-FAILED, or `--at`'s placeholder) · 3

+`/tmp`), so it reads the `TMPDIR` environment variable directly and only falls back to `java.io.tmpdir` when it
+is unset or blank, keeping the documented path real; either way, the result is resolved to an absolute,
 normalized path, since a child process such as `<worktree>/gradlew` runs inside the worktree, so a relative

+
+## Range walk
+Worktrees and bazel-diff hash files live under one fixed root, `$TMPDIR/gradle-tracer/`. At start the tracer prunes git's
+worktree list, deletes stale `wt-*` directories and stale `hashes-*.json`/`*.impacted.txt` files left by a killed run, and
+reports how many it removed; at the end it removes what it created unless `--keep-worktrees`. If bazel-diff fails once, the
+rest of the Bazel column is `n/a` and a warning goes to stderr. A commit whose Gradle run fails is `BUILD-FAILED`; the next
+commit is compared against the last good key.
+
+Exit codes are fully wired for the range walk: 0 no change, 1 usage error, 2 a tool failed (some row is
+`BUILD-FAILED`), 3 at least one row is `CHANGED` — and 2 wins over 3 when both apply in the same range.
+
+Before any worktree is created or any git command beyond option parsing runs, `--tsv-out <file>` is checked: the
+file's parent directory (the current directory when the path has none) must exist, be a directory and be
+writable, and the file itself must not already be an existing directory. Any violation is a usage error naming
+the path, so a bad destination fails immediately rather than after a whole range walk has run.
```

```bash
git add tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java \
        tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/RevisionRange.java \
        tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliTest.java \
        tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/RevisionRangeTest.java \
        docs/tracer.md
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
feat(tracer): walk the commit range from the CLI

Earlier tasks built the git range walker, worktree pool, Gradle and
bazel-diff providers, and the table renderer in isolation; wiring them
together here is what actually makes `trace` do something instead of
exiting 2 on every valid invocation. --tsv-out is checked for a usable
destination before any worktree or extra git command runs, so a typo'd
output path fails fast instead of after a whole range has been walked.
--at stays a placeholder (exit 2) until a later commit gives it real
behaviour, since RevisionRange.resolveOne doesn't exist yet.
EOF
```

The commit message quoted above is the real, byte-identical message — including "since RevisionRange.resolveOne
doesn't exist yet", which reads oddly given the diff above. In fact `resolveOne` *is* added in this very commit;
the message means it doesn't exist *in `Cli`* yet, i.e. nothing calls it until Commit B wires `Cli.at` to it.

Run `./gradlew :tools:tracer:test`: 215 tests (201 going into this task, plus 2 `RevisionRangeTest` cases and
12 `CliTest` cases this commit adds).

Wires `Cli.at` to `RevisionRange.resolveOne` and replaces Commit A's interim placeholder test with
`atPrintsTheKeyComposition`.

```diff
+    private int at(Options o, PrintStream out) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
+        Commit commit = new RevisionRange(runner, repo).resolveOne(o.at());
+        try (WorktreePool pool = new WorktreePool(runner, repo, scratch, o.keepWorktrees())) {
+            TaskKey key = keys.keyFor(pool.checkout(commit), o.taskPath());
+            out.println(key.taskPath() + " at " + commit.shortSha() + " " + commit.subject());
+            out.println("key: " + key.key());
+            out.println("implementation: " + key.implementation());
+            key.valueFingerprints().forEach((property, hash) -> out.println("value " + property + ": " + hash));
+            key.fileFingerprints().forEach((property, files) -> {
+                out.println("files " + property + ":");
+                files.forEach((path, hash) -> out.println("  " + KeyComparator.displayName(path) + "  " + hash));
+            });
+        }
+        return EXIT_OK;
+    }
 }
```

#### Commit C — `fix(tracer): lock the scratch root so concurrent runs cannot sweep each other`

Why: a second run on the same scratch root sweeps the first run's live `wt-*` worktrees and hash files, since
both `WorktreePool`'s sweep and `Cli.deleteScratchFiles` assume the root is exclusively theirs for the run's
duration.

Fix: before any sweep or worktree work, for both the range walk and `--at`, create the scratch root and take an
exclusive `FileChannel.tryLock()` on `<scratch>/.lock`, held for the whole run via try-with-resources. A `null`
result (locked by another process) or an `OverlappingFileLockException` (locked elsewhere in this same JVM) is a
usage error naming the scratch root. `.lock` is never swept: `WorktreePool`'s sweep only matches `wt-*`
directories and `Cli.deleteScratchFiles` only matches `hashes-*`/`*.impacted.txt` files, so neither one touches it.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
index 337c93b..5f479ea 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
@@ -2,8 +2,12 @@ package io.joeyang.tracer.cli;
 
 import java.io.IOException;
 import java.io.PrintStream;
+import java.nio.channels.FileChannel;
+import java.nio.channels.FileLock;
+import java.nio.channels.OverlappingFileLockException;
 import java.nio.file.Files;
 import java.nio.file.Path;
+import java.nio.file.StandardOpenOption;
 import java.time.Duration;
 import java.util.ArrayList;
 import java.util.List;
@@ -206,11 +210,10 @@ public final class Cli {
             if (!Files.isDirectory(repo.resolve("app").resolve(o.app()))) {
                 throw new UsageException("unknown app '" + o.app() + "': expected a directory app/" + o.app());
             }
-            if (o.at() != null) {
-                return at(o, out);
+            if (o.at() == null) {
+                checkTsvOutWritable(o);
             }
-            checkTsvOutWritable(o);
-            return range(o, out, err);
+            return withScratchLock(o, out, err);
         } catch (UsageException e) {
             return usage(err, e.getMessage());
         } catch (ProviderException e) {
@@ -219,6 +222,39 @@ public final class Cli {
         }
     }
 
+    /**
+     * Locks the scratch root for the whole run, before any sweep or worktree work starts: two trace runs sharing
+     * a root would otherwise sweep each other's live {@code wt-*} worktrees and hash files out from under one
+     * another. {@code tryLock()} returns null for a lock held by another process, and throws
+     * {@link OverlappingFileLockException} for one held elsewhere in this same JVM (the case the tests exercise);
+     * both mean the root is busy.
+     */
+    private int withScratchLock(Options o, PrintStream out, PrintStream err) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
+        try {
+            Files.createDirectories(scratch);
+        } catch (IOException e) {
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot create scratch root " + scratch + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
+        }
+        try (FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(scratch.resolve(".lock"), StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE)) {
+            FileLock lock;
+            try {
+                lock = channel.tryLock();
+            } catch (OverlappingFileLockException e) {
+                lock = null;
+            }
+            if (lock == null) {
+                return usage(err, "another trace is using " + scratch + "; wait for it or set TMPDIR to another directory");
+            }
+            try {
+                return o.at() != null ? at(o, out) : range(o, out, err);
+            } finally {
+                lock.release();
+            }
+        } catch (IOException e) {
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot lock scratch root " + scratch + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
+        }
+    }
+
     /**
      * Checked before any worktree is created or any git command beyond option parsing runs, so a bad
      * {@code --tsv-out} path fails fast instead of after a whole range walk has already run.
```

```diff
 - `WorktreePool` → sweeps stale worktree directories *before* pruning (see below), then `git worktree add --detach <root>/wt-<sha7> <sha>` per commit. `close()` and a shutdown hook run `git worktree remove --force`; a failed removal is a warning, not a thrown exception, so one bad worktree doesn't stop the rest from being cleaned up.
 
 ### Worktree root rules
-- The sweep at construction only ever touches directories directly under the pool's root whose name is exactly `wt-` plus 7 lowercase hex characters; anything else there (a stray file, an unrelated directory) is left alone.
-- Two `WorktreePool`s must never point at the same root at the same time: the sweep-then-prune startup sequence and `wt-<sha7>` naming assume the root is exclusively theirs for the run's duration, and a second pool's sweep would delete worktrees the first one is actively using.
+- The sweep at construction only ever touches directories directly under the pool's root whose name is exactly `wt-` plus 7 lowercase hex characters; anything else there (a stray file, an unrelated directory, `.lock`) is left alone.
+- Two `WorktreePool`s must never point at the same root at the same time: the sweep-then-prune startup sequence and `wt-<sha7>` naming assume the root is exclusively theirs for the run's duration, and a second pool's sweep would delete worktrees the first one is actively using. `Cli` now enforces this (see "One run per scratch root" below) rather than relying on it just happening to be true.
 - `--keep-worktrees` leaves everything — the checked-out directories and their `git worktree` registrations — in place when the run ends; no shutdown hook is even registered, so an interrupted run leaves them too. The *next* pool opened on that root deletes whatever is there in its startup sweep (git included, via sweep-then-prune) regardless of `--keep-worktrees`; a commit `checkout()` again within the same run tears down and recreates its worktree rather than reusing it in place.
 
 ## Comparison
```
