```diff
 the path, so a bad destination fails immediately rather than after a whole range walk has run.
+
+### One run per scratch root
+Before any sweep or worktree work — for both the range walk and `--at` — `Cli` creates the scratch root and takes
+an exclusive lock on `<scratch>/.lock` (a `FileChannel.tryLock()`), held for the whole run and released when it
+ends. A second run on the same root while the first is still going fails fast as a usage error ("another trace is
+using `<scratch>`; wait for it or set `TMPDIR` to another directory") with no git command run at all, rather than
+sweeping the first run's live `wt-*` worktrees and hash files out from under it. `.lock` itself is never swept:
+`WorktreePool`'s sweep only touches `wt-*` directories, and `Cli.deleteScratchFiles` only touches `hashes-*`/
+`*.impacted.txt` files.
```

```bash
git add tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java \
        tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliTest.java \
        docs/tracer.md
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
fix(tracer): lock the scratch root so concurrent runs cannot sweep each other

A second run on the same scratch root sweeps the first run's live
wt-* worktrees and hash files out from under it, since the sweep
assumes the root is exclusively its own for the run's duration. Take
an exclusive lock on <scratch>/.lock for the whole run so a second run
fails fast instead of corrupting the first one's in-flight work.
EOF
```

Run `./gradlew :tools:tracer:test`: 218 tests (215 going into this commit, plus 3 new `CliTest` cases).

#### Commit D — `docs(tracer): document that a detached child survives a timeout`

Docs only, `docs/tracer.md`'s `DefaultProcessRunner` row/section: on timeout, `killTree` kills what it can still
see as a descendant of the process it started, then the direct child. This commit's own example of a survivor —
"a Gradle daemon, or a Bazel server the build started" — turned out to be wrong for the Gradle half: a *freshly
spawned* Gradle daemon is still a child of the process tree `killTree` walks, so it gets killed along with
everything else; the diff below is kept byte-identical to what was actually committed, and the error is
corrected later, in Commit G.

```diff
diff --git a/docs/tracer.md b/docs/tracer.md
index 321aaa8..7c32e68 100644
--- a/docs/tracer.md
+++ b/docs/tracer.md
@@ -9,7 +9,7 @@ composed from. On a usage error it exits 1 with the usage text. The `./trace` wr
 | type | meaning |
 |---|---|
 | `ProcessRunner` / `Result` | runs a command; non-zero exit is a `Result`, "could not run" is a `ProcessException` |
-| `DefaultProcessRunner` | the real `ProcessRunner`: UTF-8 stdout/stderr capture on named daemon threads (`tracer-stdout-N`/`tracer-stderr-N`), stdin from `/dev/null`, a single deadline bounding both the process wait and the drain, and a process-tree kill (not just the direct child) on timeout |
+| `DefaultProcessRunner` | the real `ProcessRunner`: UTF-8 stdout/stderr capture on named daemon threads (`tracer-stdout-N`/`tracer-stderr-N`), stdin from `/dev/null`, a single deadline bounding both the process wait and the drain, and a process-tree kill (not just the direct child) on timeout — see below for its limit |
 | `Commit` | sha + subject |
 | `TaskKey` | a task's cache key plus per-input fingerprints (`property → file → hash`), value fingerprints and implementation hash |
 | `Status` | `BASE`, `CHANGED`, `CLEAN`, `BUILD_FAILED` — the Gradle verdict |
@@ -139,3 +139,10 @@ using `<scratch>`; wait for it or set `TMPDIR` to another directory") with no gi
 sweeping the first run's live `wt-*` worktrees and hash files out from under it. `.lock` itself is never swept:
 `WorktreePool`'s sweep only touches `wt-*` directories, and `Cli.deleteScratchFiles` only touches `hashes-*`/
 `*.impacted.txt` files.
+
+### Process timeouts and detached children
+On timeout, `DefaultProcessRunner.killTree` kills every process it can still see as a descendant of the one it
+started, then the direct child itself. A process that has already detached from that tree and been reparented —
+for example a Gradle daemon, or a Bazel server started by the build — is no longer a descendant by the time
+`killTree` runs, so it survives the kill. This is by design: those servers are meant to outlive the single
+invocation that happened to start them.
```

```bash
git add docs/tracer.md
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
docs(tracer): document that a detached child survives a timeout

killTree only kills what is still a descendant of the process it
started; a Gradle daemon or Bazel server the build spawned and that
has since detached is no longer in that set by the time a timeout
fires, so it survives. Record this as intended behaviour rather than
leave it undocumented and looking like an oversight.
EOF
```

Run `./gradlew :tools:tracer:test`: still 218 (docs-only commit). Run `bazel test //...`: 23/23. Run
`git diff --exit-code MODULE.bazel.lock` to confirm the lockfile didn't drift.

#### Commit E — `fix(tracer): resolve revisions before taking the scratch lock`

Why: spec §9 says an unknown `--from`/`--to`/`--at` exits 1 with nothing created, but since Commit C, `Cli.run`
created the scratch root and `<scratch>/.lock` before resolving the revision (`RevisionRange.resolve`/
`resolveOne` ran inside `range`/`at`, which only ran after the lock was taken), so a bad revision still left
`.lock` behind. Found by spec review.

Fix: resolve the revision(s) first — that's read-only git against the repo, not the scratch root, so it needs no
lock — then create the scratch root and take the lock, and only then sweep and walk worktrees. The `--tsv-out`
pre-check stays first, before resolution, same as before.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
index 5f479ea..474aa31 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
@@ -210,10 +210,15 @@ public final class Cli {
             if (!Files.isDirectory(repo.resolve("app").resolve(o.app()))) {
                 throw new UsageException("unknown app '" + o.app() + "': expected a directory app/" + o.app());
             }
-            if (o.at() == null) {
-                checkTsvOutWritable(o);
+            // Revision resolution is read-only git, so it needs no lock; resolving before the lock means an
+            // unknown --from/--to/--at (spec §9) exits 1 with no scratch root or .lock file left behind.
+            if (o.at() != null) {
+                Commit commit = new RevisionRange(runner, repo).resolveOne(o.at());
+                return withScratchLock(err, () -> at(o, out, commit));
             }
-            return withScratchLock(o, out, err);
+            checkTsvOutWritable(o);
+            List<Commit> commits = new RevisionRange(runner, repo).resolve(o.from(), o.to());
+            return withScratchLock(err, () -> range(o, out, err, commits));
         } catch (UsageException e) {
             return usage(err, e.getMessage());
         } catch (ProviderException e) {
@@ -222,6 +227,12 @@ public final class Cli {
         }
     }
 
+    /** The work done once the scratch root is locked: either {@link #at} or {@link #range}, with its revisions already resolved. */
+    @FunctionalInterface
+    private interface LockedWork {
+        int run() throws UsageException, ProviderException;
+    }
+
     /**
      * Locks the scratch root for the whole run, before any sweep or worktree work starts: two trace runs sharing
      * a root would otherwise sweep each other's live {@code wt-*} worktrees and hash files out from under one
@@ -229,7 +240,7 @@ public final class Cli {
      * {@link OverlappingFileLockException} for one held elsewhere in this same JVM (the case the tests exercise);
      * both mean the root is busy.
      */
-    private int withScratchLock(Options o, PrintStream out, PrintStream err) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
+    private int withScratchLock(PrintStream err, LockedWork work) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
         try {
             Files.createDirectories(scratch);
         } catch (IOException e) {
@@ -246,7 +257,7 @@ public final class Cli {
                 return usage(err, "another trace is using " + scratch + "; wait for it or set TMPDIR to another directory");
             }
             try {
-                return o.at() != null ? at(o, out) : range(o, out, err);
+                return work.run();
             } finally {
                 lock.release();
             }
@@ -279,8 +290,7 @@ public final class Cli {
         }
     }
 
-    private int range(Options o, PrintStream out, PrintStream err) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
-        List<Commit> commits = new RevisionRange(runner, repo).resolve(o.from(), o.to());
+    private int range(Options o, PrintStream out, PrintStream err, List<Commit> commits) throws ProviderException {
         int swept = deleteScratchFiles();
         if (swept > 0) {
             err.println("trace: removed " + swept + " stale scratch file(s) under " + scratch);
@@ -367,8 +377,7 @@ public final class Cli {
         return count;
     }
 
-    private int at(Options o, PrintStream out) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
-        Commit commit = new RevisionRange(runner, repo).resolveOne(o.at());
+    private int at(Options o, PrintStream out, Commit commit) throws ProviderException {
         try (WorktreePool pool = new WorktreePool(runner, repo, scratch, o.keepWorktrees())) {
             TaskKey key = keys.keyFor(pool.checkout(commit), o.taskPath());
             out.println(key.taskPath() + " at " + commit.shortSha() + " " + commit.subject());
```

#### Commit G — `docs(tracer): correct the detached-child example and make the plan's commit commands copy-safe`

Why: Commit D's `docs/tracer.md` example of a process that survives a timeout — "a Gradle daemon, or a Bazel
server started by the build" — is half wrong (found by spec review). A *freshly spawned* Gradle daemon's parent
is the `gradlew` client JVM that `killTree` started, so it's still a descendant and gets killed with everything
else; only a daemon *reused from an earlier invocation* is already detached by the time this run starts, so only
that one survives. The Bazel half was already correct: a Bazel server daemonizes on start and gets reparented to
a subreaper, so it's never a descendant either.

Also: every Task 13 commit block above used `git commit -m "…"` with the real message inline, and at least one
message contains a backticked word (`` `trace` `` in Commit A's). A shell that runs the block as typed — e.g.
pasted into a terminal — would read `` `trace` `` inside those double quotes as a command substitution, not
literal text. Every block above (A–F) has already been switched to `git commit -F - <<'EOF' ... EOF`, which
takes the message from a quoted heredoc and so can't expand anything inside it; this commit's own block uses the
same form. No message text changed.

```diff
diff --git a/docs/tracer.md b/docs/tracer.md
index d0ea4ff..62d8597 100644
--- a/docs/tracer.md
+++ b/docs/tracer.md
@@ -148,7 +148,9 @@ directories, and `Cli.deleteScratchFiles` only touches `hashes-*`/`*.impacted.tx
 
 ### Process timeouts and detached children
 On timeout, `DefaultProcessRunner.killTree` kills every process it can still see as a descendant of the one it
-started, then the direct child itself. A process that has already detached from that tree and been reparented —
-for example a Gradle daemon, or a Bazel server started by the build — is no longer a descendant by the time
-`killTree` runs, so it survives the kill. This is by design: those servers are meant to outlive the single
-invocation that happened to start them.
+started, then the direct child itself. A freshly spawned Gradle daemon is still a child of that process tree (its
+parent is the `gradlew` client JVM `killTree` started), so it is killed along with everything else. What survives
+is a process that had already detached and been reparented *before* this invocation started it: a Gradle daemon
+reused from an earlier invocation, or a Bazel server (which daemonizes on start) — neither is a descendant of the
+process `killTree` is walking, so it is never in scope to kill. This is by design: those servers are meant to
+outlive the single invocation that happened to start or reuse them.
```

```bash
git add docs/tracer.md docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-build-input-tracer.md
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
docs(tracer): correct the detached-child example and make the plan's commit commands copy-safe

Commit D's example of a process that survives killTree's timeout kill
was half wrong: a freshly spawned Gradle daemon is still a child of
the process tree killTree walks, so it gets killed too. Only a daemon
reused from an earlier invocation, or a Bazel server (which daemonizes
on start), is already detached and so survives. Separately, the plan's
Task 13 commit blocks quoted commit messages inline in "git commit -m",
and at least one message has a backticked word that a shell would read
as a command substitution if the block were pasted as-is; switch every
block to "git commit -F - <<'EOF'" so the message is taken literally.
EOF
```

#### Commit G2 — `docs(tracer): fix a Gradle-oracle sentence left stale by Task 13`

Why: spec review caught one more stale sentence while reviewing Commit G: `docs/tracer.md`'s Gradle-oracle
section said project dependencies "appear as class directories on the compile classpath and as jars on the
runtime classpath, which *later commits* use to name a culprit". The sentence dates back to Task 7
(`cfe5a5c`, written while culprit naming genuinely was future work); it went stale at Task 10 (`0492bd0`),
which implemented that naming in `KeyComparator.displayName`, and nothing updated the sentence then. Name the
method instead of a vague forward reference. (The 387190a commit that made this fix called the sentence "left
stale by Task 13" in its subject line — that's the commit review round that *caught* it, not the one that made
it stale; the staleness dates to Task 10, three tasks earlier.)

This belongs conceptually inside Commit G (same root cause, same reviewer pass), but lands as its own commit
instead of an amendment to G: the branch is shared with other agents and the no-amend rule applies regardless of
how small or related the fix is.

```diff
diff --git a/docs/tracer.md b/docs/tracer.md
index 62d8597..e57cdbd 100644
--- a/docs/tracer.md
+++ b/docs/tracer.md
@@ -30,8 +30,8 @@ BUILD-FAILED row and a CHANGED row.
 - `Appending input value fingerprint for '<property>' ...: <hash>` → scalar inputs such as `options.compilerArgs`
 - `Appending input file fingerprints for '<property>' ...: <hash> - <NORMALIZATION>{<path>=<'normalized'|IGNORED> / <hash|DIR|MISSING>, ...}` → per-file hashes
   (a directory or a deleted file prints `DIR`/`MISSING` in place of a hash; project dependencies otherwise appear as class
-  directories on the compile classpath and as jars on the runtime classpath, which later commits use to name a culprit like
-  `core classes` under `compileJava` versus `core.jar` under `fingerprint`)
+  directories on the compile classpath and as jars on the runtime classpath, which `KeyComparator.displayName` names as a
+  culprit like `core classes` under `compileJava` versus `core.jar` under `fingerprint`)
 - `Build cache key for task '<path>' is <hash>` → the key, which closes the block
 - `Non-cacheable because <reason>` (jar, installDist, …) also closes the block with no key; the provider reports the reason
   instead of a silent `clean`
```

#### Commit I — `test(tracer): prove a bad revision creates no scratch root`

Why: the two "…LeavesNoScratchRoot" tests added in Commit E only asserted that `.lock` was absent; they didn't
assert that the scratch root *directory* itself was never created, and there was no equivalent test for an
unknown `--to` or for an empty range (`--from`/`--to` resolving to the same commit) — spec §9's "nothing
created" promise covers all of these the same way an unknown `--from` or `--at` does.

#### Commit J — `docs(tracer): fix four accuracy slips in the Task 13 docs and plan`

Four accuracy fixes, all docs-only, found by a further spec review round:

a. Spec §14's T4 row said the fix "enforced §9's single-run-per-scratch-root rule" — but §9 (read before writing
   this) has no such rule; its only relevant row is "unknown `--from`/`--to`, unknown app, empty range | message
   naming the bad value, exit 1, nothing created". T4 is reworded to cite only that, and to describe the
   scratch-root lock as what it actually is: an implementation safeguard against two concurrent runs sharing a
   root, not something §9 specifies.

b. `docs/tracer.md`'s "`Cli.run` does its work in a fixed order" paragraph said `--tsv-out` is pre-checked
   "before anything else beyond option parsing" — but `Cli.java:210` checks the app name against `app/<app>`
   first, before the `--tsv-out` check ever runs. Reworded to state the real order: app check, then (for a
   range) the `--tsv-out` pre-check, then revision resolution, then the scratch lock.

c. This plan's Commit G and Commit I blocks each land a change to this very plan file in the same commit as a
   code/docs change, but their `git add` lines only listed the other file(s) — self-reference is awkward to get
   right while drafting a block that describes its own commit. Checked every Task 13 block's `git add` list
   against `git show --name-only --format= <sha>` for the real commit (a quick script, not by eye); only G and I
   were short a path, both missing this same plan file. Both are now fixed to list every file the real commit
   touched.

d. The 387190a commit that fixed `docs/tracer.md`'s stale "later commits use to name a culprit" sentence has the
   subject "fix a Gradle-oracle sentence left stale by Task 13" — Task 13 is the review round that *caught* it,
   not the one that made it stale. The sentence was written in Task 7 (`cfe5a5c`), when culprit naming genuinely
   was future work, and went stale in Task 10 (`0492bd0`), which implemented that naming in
   `KeyComparator.displayName` without updating this sentence. Commit G2's own "Why" paragraph in this plan
   (which already named `KeyComparator.displayName` correctly) is corrected to say so, and now also calls out the
   387190a subject's misattribution; `387190a`'s own commit message is left untouched — it's real history, not
   rewritten here.

nit. `docs/tracer.md`'s "Process timeouts and detached children" section said a Bazel server "had already
   detached and been reparented *before* this invocation started it" — true of a *reused* Gradle daemon, not of
   a Bazel server this run starts itself: that one detaches *during* the run, not before it. Reworded to give the
   two cases their own, accurate reasons.

```diff
diff --git a/docs/tracer.md b/docs/tracer.md
index e57cdbd..0ff48ea 100644
--- a/docs/tracer.md
+++ b/docs/tracer.md
@@ -126,14 +126,14 @@ commit is compared against the last good key.
 Exit codes are fully wired for the range walk: 0 no change, 1 usage error, 2 a tool failed (some row is
 `BUILD-FAILED`), 3 at least one row is `CHANGED` — and 2 wins over 3 when both apply in the same range.
 
-`Cli.run` does its work in a fixed order: `--tsv-out <file>` is pre-checked (parent directory must exist, be a
-directory and be writable; the target must not already be a directory) before anything else beyond option
-parsing; then the revision(s) are resolved (`RevisionRange.resolve` for a range, `resolveOne` for `--at`) — this
-is read-only git, so it needs no lock, and a usage error here (unknown revision, empty range) exits 1 with no
-scratch root or `.lock` file ever created; only once that succeeds does the scratch root get created, locked
-(see "One run per scratch root" below), swept, and the worktree walk begin. Any violation along the way is a
-usage error naming the offending path or revision, so a bad input fails immediately rather than after a whole
-range walk has run.
+`Cli.run` does its work in a fixed order: the app name is checked against `app/<app>` first (a usage error here
+needs no scratch root or git at all); then, for a range, `--tsv-out <file>` is pre-checked (parent directory
+must exist, be a directory and be writable; the target must not already be a directory); then the revision(s)
+are resolved (`RevisionRange.resolve` for a range, `resolveOne` for `--at`) — this is read-only git, so it needs
+no lock, and a usage error here (unknown revision, empty range) exits 1 with no scratch root or `.lock` file
+ever created; only once that succeeds does the scratch root get created, locked (see "One run per scratch root"
+below), swept, and the worktree walk begin. Any violation along the way is a usage error naming the offending
+app, path or revision, so a bad input fails immediately rather than after a whole range walk has run.
 
 ### One run per scratch root
 Before any sweep or worktree work — for both the range walk and `--at`, and only after their revisions are
@@ -149,8 +149,9 @@ directories, and `Cli.deleteScratchFiles` only touches `hashes-*`/`*.impacted.tx
 ### Process timeouts and detached children
 On timeout, `DefaultProcessRunner.killTree` kills every process it can still see as a descendant of the one it
 started, then the direct child itself. A freshly spawned Gradle daemon is still a child of that process tree (its
-parent is the `gradlew` client JVM `killTree` started), so it is killed along with everything else. What survives
-is a process that had already detached and been reparented *before* this invocation started it: a Gradle daemon
-reused from an earlier invocation, or a Bazel server (which daemonizes on start) — neither is a descendant of the
-process `killTree` is walking, so it is never in scope to kill. This is by design: those servers are meant to
-outlive the single invocation that happened to start or reuse them.
+parent is the `gradlew` client JVM `killTree` started), so it is killed along with everything else. Two kinds of
+process are never in scope to kill, for two different reasons: a Gradle daemon reused from an earlier invocation
+was already detached and reparented before this run even started it, so it was never this process tree's child to
+begin with; a Bazel server, which daemonizes as soon as it starts, detaches *during* this run, before `killTree`
+would ever need to act, so it is never a descendant of the client that launched it either. This is by design:
+those servers are meant to outlive the single invocation that happened to start or reuse them.
```

(found by the Task 10 code-quality review) | fixed: the worktree root is stripped first (sharing `WorktreePool`'s `wt-<sha7>` naming), then the module boundary is the *first* `/src/` in what remains; "build logic" sorts first so it is never hidden by the culprit cap; TSV fields are tab/CR/LF-escaped; table columns are truncated/padded by code point; culprit-less rows are trimmed; `appName` falls back to the full task path. Supersedes F14's fix, not §5/§8's design intent | plan Task 10 |
```diff
-| T4 | spec §9 says a bad revision exits 1 with nothing created; after Task 13 commit C, `Cli` created the scratch root and `<scratch>/.lock` before resolving `--from`/`--to`/`--at`, so an unknown revision left `.lock` behind (found by spec review) | fixed: revision resolution (read-only git, no lock needed) now runs before the scratch root is created or locked. §9's single-run-per-scratch-root rule is enforced by a `FileChannel` lock on `<scratch>/.lock`, taken after revision resolution; contention exits 1 | plan Task 13 (commit E) |
+| T4 | §9 says a bad revision exits 1 with nothing created; after Task 13 commit C, `Cli` created the scratch root and `<scratch>/.lock` before resolving `--from`/`--to`/`--at`, so an unknown revision left `.lock` behind, violating that row (found by spec review) | fixed: revision resolution (read-only git, no lock needed) now runs before the scratch root is created or locked, restoring §9's "nothing created" guarantee. (The scratch-root lock itself, a `FileChannel` lock on `<scratch>/.lock` guarding against two concurrent runs sharing a root, is an implementation safeguard added in commit C — §9 doesn't specify it; taking it after revision resolution is what keeps the two consistent.) | plan Task 13 (commit E) |
```

#### Commit K — `fix(tracer): report unexpected runtime exceptions as exit 2`

Why: a provider or a filesystem call can throw an unchecked exception `Cli.run` wasn't catching — quality
review confirmed the trigger experimentally: `WorktreePool.deleteRecursively`'s `Files.walk` throws
`UncheckedIOException` on an unreadable subdirectory, and `Cli.deleteScratchFiles`'s `Files.list(...).toList()`
can throw the same way. Previously that escaped `run` as a raw stack trace with whatever exit code the JVM
happened to pick, instead of the tool's own exit-2 convention.

Fix: a final `catch (RuntimeException e)` in `run`, after the existing `UsageException`/`ProviderException`
catches, reports it as `trace: internal error: <class>: <message>` and returns `EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE` — no stack
trace printed.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
index 474aa31..5d1f69c 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
@@ -224,6 +224,12 @@ public final class Cli {
         } catch (ProviderException e) {
             err.println("trace: " + e.getMessage());
             return EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE;
+        } catch (RuntimeException e) {
+            // A provider or a filesystem call can throw an unchecked exception we didn't anticipate (e.g.
+            // UncheckedIOException from Files.walk on an unreadable subdirectory); report it as a tool failure
+            // instead of letting it escape as a raw stack trace with the JVM's own non-zero exit code.
+            err.println("trace: internal error: " + e.getClass().getName() + ": " + e.getMessage());
+            return EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE;
         }
     }
 
```


```bash
fix(tracer): report unexpected runtime exceptions as exit 2

WorktreePool.deleteRecursively's Files.walk throws UncheckedIOException
on an unreadable subdirectory, and Cli.deleteScratchFiles' Files.list
can throw the same way; neither is caught, so either escapes run() as
a raw stack trace with an arbitrary exit code. Catch RuntimeException
as a last resort and report it the same way every other tool failure
is reported: exit 2, one line on stderr, no stack trace.
```

#### Commit L — `fix(tracer): clean up hash files when the walk aborts`

Why: the end-of-run `deleteScratchFiles()` call sat after the `WorktreePool` try-with-resources block, not in a
`finally`, so when e.g. `git worktree add` failed partway through a range (quality review's probe: the 3rd
commit), `hashes-*.json` files for the commits already processed were left behind — contradicting
`docs/tracer.md`'s "at the end it removes what it created" promise, which was only ever true for the
happy path.

Fix: wrap the `WorktreePool` block in `try Ellipsis finally { if (!keepWorktrees) deleteScratchFiles(); }`, so
the cleanup runs whether the walk finished normally or a checkout/pool failure aborted it. No partial-row output
is added for the aborted case — that's deferred; the run still just reports the failure and exits 2.

```diff
 ## Range walk
 Worktrees and bazel-diff hash files live under one fixed root, `$TMPDIR/gradle-tracer/`. At start the tracer prunes git's
 worktree list, deletes stale `wt-*` directories and stale `hashes-*.json`/`*.impacted.txt` files left by a killed run, and

+reports how many it removed; at the end — including when a checkout or provider failure aborts the walk partway
```

#### Commit M — `fix(tracer): reject a read-only --tsv-out file up front and write it atomically`

Why: the `--tsv-out` pre-check validated the parent directory but never checked an *existing* target itself —
an existing read-only file would pass the pre-check, run the whole range walk, and only then fail trying to
write it. Separately, the final write was a plain `Files.writeString`, so a write that failed partway through
(disk full, permissions revoked mid-walk) could leave the target truncated or corrupted, destroying whatever
was there before.

Fix: the pre-check now also rejects an existing, non-writable target. The write itself goes through a temp file
in the same directory plus `Files.move(..., ATOMIC_MOVE, REPLACE_EXISTING)`, deleting the temp file on failure,
so a failed write never touches the previous file.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java

 import java.nio.channels.OverlappingFileLockException;

 import java.nio.file.StandardOpenOption;
 import java.time.Duration;
 import java.util.ArrayList;

             throw new UsageException("--tsv-out '" + tsvOut + "' is a directory");
         }
+        if (Files.exists(tsvOut) && !Files.isWritable(tsvOut)) {
+            throw new UsageException("--tsv-out '" + tsvOut + "' exists and is not writable");
+        }
         Path parent = tsvOut.toAbsolutePath().getParent();
         if (parent == null) {
             parent = Path.of("").toAbsolutePath();

-            try {
-                Files.writeString(o.tsvOut(), tsv);
-            } catch (IOException e) {
-                throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + o.tsvOut() + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
-            }
+            writeTsvAtomically(o.tsvOut(), tsv);

         if (rows.stream().anyMatch(v -> v.status() == Status.BUILD_FAILED)) {

@@ -368,6 +368,35 @@ public final class Cli {

+    /**
+     * Writes {@code content} to {@code target} via a temp file plus an atomic move, so a write that fails
+     * partway through (disk full, permissions revoked mid-walk, ...) never leaves {@code target} truncated or
+     * corrupted — the previous file, if any, is untouched.
+     */
+    private void writeTsvAtomically(Path target, String content) throws ProviderException {
+        Path parent = target.toAbsolutePath().getParent();
+        if (parent == null) {
+            parent = Path.of("").toAbsolutePath();
+        }
+        Path tmp;
+        try {
+            tmp = Files.createTempFile(parent, "trace-", ".tsv.tmp");
+        } catch (IOException e) {
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + target + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
+        }
+        try {
+            Files.writeString(tmp, content);
+            Files.move(tmp, target, StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);
+        } catch (IOException e) {
+            try {
+                Files.deleteIfExists(tmp);
+            } catch (IOException ignored) {
+                // best effort
+            }
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + target + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
+        }
+    }
+

         if (!Files.isDirectory(scratch)) {
```

#### Commit N — `fix(tracer): stop the walk when interrupted`

Why: an interrupted key lookup was recorded as an ordinary `BUILD-FAILED` row and the walk carried on — but the
interrupt flag stays set (`DefaultProcessRunner` restores it before throwing), so every later process call was
going to fail the exact same way. The run limped through the rest of the range producing a wall of
`BUILD-FAILED` rows instead of the one honest signal that it was asked to stop.

Fix: after a key-lookup failure, check `Thread.currentThread().isInterrupted()`; if set, abort the walk with a
`ProviderException("interrupted", ...)` instead of recording a row and continuing. Cleanup (Commit L's
`finally`) still runs, and the flag itself is left exactly as found — nothing here clears or re-sets it.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
index 797cb73..ad6df2b 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
@@ -318,6 +318,12 @@ public final class Cli {
                     try {
                         key = Optional.of(keys.keyFor(worktree, o.taskPath()));
                     } catch (ProviderException e) {
+                        // An interrupted process call fails this one lookup but leaves the interrupt flag set, so
+                        // every later process call would fail the same way; limping on would just produce a wall
+                        // of BUILD-FAILED rows instead of the one honest signal that the run was asked to stop.
+                        if (Thread.currentThread().isInterrupted()) {
+                            throw new ProviderException("interrupted", e);
+                        }
                         error = Optional.of(e.getMessage());
                     }
                     BazelStatus bazel = BazelStatus.NONE;
```

```diff
+    @Test void interruptedKeyLookupAbortsTheWalk() throws Exception {

+        KeyProvider interrupting = (wt, task) -> {
+            calls.incrementAndGet();
+            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();

+                assertTrue(left.noneMatch(p -> p.getFileName().toString().startsWith("hashes-")),
+                        "cleanup must still run when the walk is aborted by an interrupt");
+            }
+        } finally {
+            Thread.interrupted(); // clear it, so it doesn't leak into later tests on this thread
+        }
+    }
```

#### Commit O — `fix(tracer): tidy scratch-lock handling and IO error messages`

Why: `withScratchLock` released the `FileLock` with a manual `lock.release()` in a `finally`, so if the locked
work (the range walk or `--at`) threw and `release()` also threw, the second exception replaced the first —
masking the real failure. Lock contention also went through the shared `usage()` helper, printing the full
usage block for something that isn't a command-line mistake. Separately, three `IOException` messages
(`Cli.java:247, 265, 351` before this commit) just echoed `e.getMessage()`, which for some `IOException`
subtypes is nothing more than the path already named alongside it.

Fix: hold the `FileLock` in its own try-with-resources so a failure in `release()` is suppressed onto the
work's own exception instead of replacing it (the `FileLock lock; try { lock = ch.tryLock(); } catch
(OverlappingFileLockException e) { lock = null; }` shape, feeding one `if (lock == null)` path, was already
exactly this; what changes is wrapping the non-null `lock` itself in `try (FileLock heldLock = lock) { ... }`
instead of a manual `finally`). Lock contention now prints just its own message and returns `EXIT_USAGE`
directly, skipping the usage block. A new `ioReason(IOException, Path)` helper mirrors
`BazelDiffProvider.java:122-123`'s fallback to the exception's simple class name when the message is missing or
is just the path again, applied everywhere `Cli` builds a message from an `IOException`.


```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
index ad6df2b..dd0e7da 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
@@ -251,9 +251,10 @@ public final class Cli {
         try {
             Files.createDirectories(scratch);
         } catch (IOException e) {
-            throw new ProviderException("cannot create scratch root " + scratch + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot create scratch root " + scratch + ": " + ioReason(e, scratch), e);
         }
-        try (FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(scratch.resolve(".lock"), StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE)) {
+        Path lockPath = scratch.resolve(".lock");
+        try (FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(lockPath, StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE)) {
             FileLock lock;
             try {
                 lock = channel.tryLock();
@@ -261,18 +262,33 @@ public final class Cli {
                 lock = null;
             }
             if (lock == null) {
-                return usage(err, "another trace is using " + scratch + "; wait for it or set TMPDIR to another directory");
+                // Lock contention isn't a command-line mistake, so it gets its own message, not the full usage
+                // text; the exit code still reads as a usage error (something about this invocation, not a tool
+                // failure, prevented it from running).
+                err.println("trace: another trace is using " + scratch + "; wait for it or set TMPDIR to another directory");
+                return EXIT_USAGE;
             }
-            try {
+            // try-with-resources (not a manual release() in a finally) so that if work.run() throws and release()
+            // also fails, release()'s IOException is suppressed onto work's exception rather than replacing it.
+            try (FileLock heldLock = lock) {
                 return work.run();
-            } finally {
-                lock.release();
             }
         } catch (IOException e) {
-            throw new ProviderException("cannot lock scratch root " + scratch + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot lock scratch root " + lockPath + ": " + ioReason(e, lockPath), e);
         }
     }
 
+    /**
+     * {@code e.getMessage()}, falling back to the exception's simple class name when the message is missing or is
+     * just the path again (some {@link IOException} subtypes report nothing more useful than that), so the
+     * reported reason is never empty or redundant with the path already named alongside it. Mirrors
+     * {@code BazelDiffProvider}'s identical fallback for the same class of message.
+     */
+    private static String ioReason(IOException e, Path path) {
+        String message = e.getMessage();
+        return (message == null || message.equals(path.toString())) ? e.getClass().getSimpleName() : message;
+    }
+
     /**
      * Checked before any worktree is created or any git command beyond option parsing runs, so a bad
      * {@code --tsv-out} path fails fast instead of after a whole range walk has already run.
@@ -388,7 +404,7 @@ public final class Cli {
         try {
             tmp = Files.createTempFile(parent, "trace-", ".tsv.tmp");
         } catch (IOException e) {
-            throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + target + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + target + ": " + ioReason(e, parent), e);
         }
         try {
             Files.writeString(tmp, content);
@@ -399,7 +415,7 @@ public final class Cli {
             } catch (IOException ignored) {
                 // best effort
             }
-            throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + target + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + target + ": " + ioReason(e, target), e);
         }
     }
```

```bash
fix(tracer): tidy scratch-lock handling and IO error messages
```

#### Commit P — `fix(tracer): refuse a scratch root that is not private to the user`

Security finding from quality review: `$TMPDIR/gradle-tracer` is a predictable path (spec §9's fixed scratch
root), often under a shared `/tmp`. Another local user could pre-create it and tamper with the worktrees whose
`gradlew` this tool executes, or hold `.lock` to block every run — the fixed-path design (deliberately kept,
per §9) otherwise has no defence against that.

Fix: `ensurePrivateScratchRoot`, called before anything else in `withScratchLock`, creates a fresh root
`rwx------` (`PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute`, which sets the mode explicitly rather than relying on the
process umask — the three existing test fixtures that pre-created `scratch` with `Files.createDirectories`
picked up this environment's `0002` umask as `rwxrwxr-x` and so now legitimately trip the new check; they
switch to a `createPrivateScratch()` helper that matches what `Cli` itself creates). An *existing* root must
already be owned by the current user with no group/other write bit, checked via `checkScratchRootIsPrivate`,
or the run exits 1 naming the path. Ownership by another user can't be tested without root, so only the
permission-bits branch has a test; the test comments say so.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
index dd0e7da..7e957ef 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
@@ -9,6 +9,8 @@ import java.nio.file.Files;
 import java.nio.file.Path;
 import java.nio.file.StandardCopyOption;
 import java.nio.file.StandardOpenOption;
+import java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission;
+import java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions;
 import java.time.Duration;
 import java.util.ArrayList;
 import java.util.List;
@@ -248,11 +250,7 @@ public final class Cli {
      * both mean the root is busy.
      */
     private int withScratchLock(PrintStream err, LockedWork work) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
-        try {
-            Files.createDirectories(scratch);
-        } catch (IOException e) {
-            throw new ProviderException("cannot create scratch root " + scratch + ": " + ioReason(e, scratch), e);
-        }
+        ensurePrivateScratchRoot();
         Path lockPath = scratch.resolve(".lock");
         try (FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(lockPath, StandardOpenOption.CREATE, StandardOpenOption.WRITE)) {
             FileLock lock;
@@ -278,6 +276,45 @@ public final class Cli {
         }
     }
 
+    private static final Set<PosixFilePermission> PRIVATE_SCRATCH_PERMISSIONS = PosixFilePermissions.fromString("rwx------");
+
+    /**
+     * $TMPDIR/gradle-tracer is a predictable path, often under a shared {@code /tmp}: another local user could
+     * pre-create it and tamper with the worktrees whose {@code gradlew} this tool executes, or hold {@code .lock}
+     * to block every run. A fresh root is created {@code rwx------}; an existing one must already be owned by
+     * the current user with no group/other write bit, or the run refuses to touch it.
+     */
+    private void ensurePrivateScratchRoot() throws UsageException, ProviderException {
+        if (Files.isDirectory(scratch)) {
+            checkScratchRootIsPrivate();
+            return;
+        }
+        try {
+            Path parent = scratch.getParent();
+            if (parent != null) {
+                Files.createDirectories(parent);
+            }
+            Files.createDirectory(scratch, PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(PRIVATE_SCRATCH_PERMISSIONS));
+        } catch (IOException e) {
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot create scratch root " + scratch + ": " + ioReason(e, scratch), e);
+        }
+    }
+
+    private void checkScratchRootIsPrivate() throws UsageException, ProviderException {
+        try {
+            Set<PosixFilePermission> perms = Files.getPosixFilePermissions(scratch);
+            boolean groupOrOtherWrite = perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_WRITE)
+                    || perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE);
+            boolean notOwnedByMe = !Files.getOwner(scratch).getName().equals(System.getProperty("user.name"));
+            if (groupOrOtherWrite || notOwnedByMe) {
+                throw new UsageException("scratch root " + scratch
+                        + " is not private to you; remove it or set TMPDIR to a private directory");
+            }
+        } catch (IOException e) {
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot check scratch root " + scratch + ": " + ioReason(e, scratch), e);
+        }
+    }
+
     /**
      * {@code e.getMessage()}, falling back to the exception's simple class name when the message is missing or is
      * just the path again (some {@link IOException} subtypes report nothing more useful than that), so the
```

(The scratch-root lock itself, a `FileChannel` lock on `<scratch>/.lock` guarding against two concurrent runs sharing a root, is an implementation safeguard added in commit C — §9 doesn't specify it; taking it after revision resolution is what keeps the two consistent.) | plan Task 13 (commit E) |
```diff
+| T5 | `$TMPDIR/gradle-tracer` (spec §9's fixed scratch root) is a predictable path, often under a shared `/tmp`; another local user could pre-create it and tamper with the worktrees whose `gradlew` this tool executes, or hold `.lock` to block every run (found by quality review) | fixed: a fresh scratch root is created `rwx------`; an existing one must already be owned by the current user with no group/other write bit, or the run refuses to touch it, exiting 1 with a message naming the path. #### Commit Q — `fix(tracer): surface git log failures in resolveOne`
```

Why: `resolveOne` called `run("git", "log", "-1", FORMAT, sha)` and fed its stdout straight to `parse()` without
checking the exit code, unlike `resolve()` (which already checks the equivalent base-commit `git log` call).
A failing `git log` there — e.g. a corrupt object — would feed `parse()` an empty string and surface as a
confusing "unexpected git log line" `ProviderException` instead of git's own error.

Fix: check `result.ok()` the same way `resolve()` does, before parsing.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/RevisionRange.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/RevisionRange.java
index 6a6fb5c..3cd4a98 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/RevisionRange.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/RevisionRange.java
@@ -61,7 +61,11 @@ public final class RevisionRange {
     /** A single revision as a Commit (for {@code --at}). */
     public Commit resolveOne(String rev) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
         String sha = revParse(rev);
-        return parse(run("git", "log", "-1", FORMAT, sha).stdout().strip());
+        ProcessRunner.Result result = run("git", "log", "-1", FORMAT, sha);
+        if (!result.ok()) {
+            throw new ProviderException("git log failed for '" + sha + "': " + result.firstErrorLine());
+        }
+        return parse(result.stdout().strip());
     }
 
     private String revParse(String rev) throws UsageException, ProviderException {
```

```diff
+        assertTrue(e.getMessage().contains("bad object"), e.getMessage());
```

```diff
@@ -58,7 +58,9 @@ any of these failures to `BazelStatus.NOT_AVAILABLE` for the rest of the range (
 and prints a warning to stderr once, the first time bazel-diff fails.
 
 ## Git layer
-- `RevisionRange.resolve(from, to)` → `[from, …from..to oldest first]`, following the first-parent line so adjacent rows are parent and child even across merges. `from` must be an ancestor of `to` on that first-parent line (each row's own first parent, from `%P`, must equal the previous row's sha); a side-branch commit pulled in by a merge is a `UsageException` naming both shas, not a silent mismatch. Unknown revision (`git rev-parse --verify --quiet` exit 1) or an empty range → `UsageException`; any other git failure (not runnable, exit 128, a malformed `git log` line) → `ProviderException`, naming the offending line where there is one. `RevisionRange.resolveOne(rev)` resolves and describes a single revision the same way, for `--at`.
+- `RevisionRange.resolve(from, to)` → `[from, …from..to oldest first]`, following the first-parent line so adjacent rows are parent and child even across merges. `from` must be an ancestor of `to` on that first-parent line (each row's own first parent, from `%P`, must equal the previous row's sha); a side-branch commit pulled in by a merge is a `UsageException` naming both shas, not a silent mismatch. Unknown revision (`git rev-parse --verify --quiet` exit 1) or an empty range → `UsageException`; any other git failure (not runnable, exit 128, a malformed `git log` line) → `ProviderException`, naming the offending line where there is one. `RevisionRange.resolveOne(rev)` resolves and describes a single revision the same way, for `--at`, including the
+`git log` exit-code check: a non-zero exit there is a `ProviderException` naming git's own first stderr line,
+not something `parse` has to make sense of.
 - `WorktreePool` → sweeps stale worktree directories *before* pruning (see below), then `git worktree add --detach <root>/wt-<sha7> <sha>` per commit. `close()` and a shutdown hook run `git worktree remove --force`; a failed removal is a warning, not a thrown exception, so one bad worktree doesn't stop the rest from being cleaned up.
 
 ### Worktree root rules
```

```bash
fix(tracer): surface git log failures in resolveOne

resolveOne fed git log's stdout straight to parse() without checking
the exit code, unlike resolve()'s equivalent base-commit lookup. A
failing git log there surfaced as a confusing "unexpected git log
line" instead of git's own error. Check result.ok() the same way
```

#### Commit R — `test(tracer): cover the remaining exit paths`

Test-only: quality review found gaps in `CliTest`'s exit-path coverage and a couple of loose assertions.

Added: a range where every commit's key is identical (`EXIT_OK`), `--at` with a failing key lookup
(`EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE`), and `--keep-worktrees` on the range path (no `git worktree remove` call, and this run's
hash files still present — the fake runner never touches a real filesystem for `git worktree add` itself, so
worktree-directory existence isn't the right thing to assert here; not removing them is).

Tightened: `tsvGoesToStdoutWithoutTable` now asserts the exit code, not just the output. `bazelFailureDegradesToNotAvailable`
now asserts the third row (not just the second) is also `n/a`, and that the warning prints exactly once for the
whole run, not once per remaining commit. `tsvOutWithUnwritableParentIsAUsageError`'s loose
`contains(file) || contains(dir)` becomes an exact match on the real message, which already names both.

No assume/skip for root was added, as instructed — none of this needed one.

#### Commit S — `docs(tracer): align the range-walk docs and comments with the code`

Why: quality review found the "Range walk" section's sweep order didn't match the code (it sweeps hash files,
then `wt-*` directories, then prunes — the doc had prune first), used `.json` where the code actually matches
any extension on a `hashes-*` name, and the exit-2 list omitted git failures, scratch/lock IO failures and the
TSV write failure, listing only `BUILD-FAILED`, the internal-error case and the interrupt case. Three comments
also referenced task numbers or test-suite internals that date the prose instead of describing current
behaviour, and a `//` line comment sat stacked directly above a separate `/**` Javadoc on `DEFAULT_SCRATCH`.

Decision on the sweep order: described the code as it is (hash files, then `wt-*` directories, then prune)
rather than reordering the code to match the doc. The existing order already makes sense — Cli's own,
cheaply-checked hash files first, then handing off to `WorktreePool`'s own sweep-then-prune sequence — and nothing
in this review asked for a behaviour change, only an accurate description of one.

Removed history: `RevisionRangeTest`'s "from Task 9" (the referenced tests are in the same file, task numbers
aren't needed to find them); `docs/tracer.md`'s "`Cli` now enforces this" (the enforcement is just what `Cli`
does, not news); `Cli.java`'s "(the case the tests exercise)" (a true fact about the test suite, not about the
method). Merged the stacked `//`/`/**` comments on `DEFAULT_SCRATCH` into one Javadoc.

```diff
 ### Worktree root rules
 - The sweep at construction only ever touches directories directly under the pool's root whose name is exactly `wt-` plus 7 lowercase hex characters; anything else there (a stray file, an unrelated directory, `.lock`) is left alone.
-- Two `WorktreePool`s must never point at the same root at the same time: the sweep-then-prune startup sequence and `wt-<sha7>` naming assume the root is exclusively theirs for the run's duration, and a second pool's sweep would delete worktrees the first one is actively using. `Cli` now enforces this (see "One run per scratch root" below) rather than relying on it just happening to be true.
+- Two `WorktreePool`s must never point at the same root at the same time: the sweep-then-prune startup sequence and `wt-<sha7>` naming assume the root is exclusively theirs for the run's duration, and a second pool's sweep would delete worktrees the first one is actively using. `Cli` enforces this (see "One run per scratch root" below) rather than relying on it just happening to be true.
 - `--keep-worktrees` leaves everything — the checked-out directories and their `git worktree` registrations — in place when the run ends; no shutdown hook is even registered, so an interrupted run leaves them too. The *next* pool opened on that root deletes whatever is there in its startup sweep (git included, via sweep-then-prune) regardless of `--keep-worktrees`; a commit `checkout()` again within the same run tears down and recreates its worktree rather than reusing it in place.

+Worktrees and bazel-diff hash files live under one fixed root, `$TMPDIR/gradle-tracer/`. At start the tracer
+deletes stale scratch files left by a killed run — any file whose name starts with `hashes-` (any extension,
+not just `.json`) or ends with `.impacted.txt` — and reports how many; only then does `WorktreePool` delete
+stale `wt-*` directories and prune git's worktree list, in that order (hash files first, since they're Cli's own
+and cheap to check; worktree directories and git's own bookkeeping after). At the end — including when a
+checkout or provider failure aborts the walk partway through — it removes what it created unless
+`--keep-worktrees`, which keeps the worktrees themselves (their `git worktree` registrations too) and every
+hash file this run wrote. If bazel-diff fails once, the rest of the Bazel column is `n/a` and exactly one
+warning goes to stderr for the whole run, not one per remaining commit. A commit whose Gradle run fails is
+`BUILD-FAILED`; the next commit is compared against the last good key.
+
+Exit codes are fully wired for the range walk: 0 no change, 1 usage error, 2 a tool failed — any of: some row is
+`BUILD-FAILED`; a `git` failure outside revision resolution (e.g. a `git worktree add` that fails mid-walk); an
+IO failure creating or checking the scratch root, or opening `.lock`; a `--tsv-out` write failure (the previous
+file, if any, is left untouched — see "The scratch root must be private" and the TSV atomic-write note above);
+an unexpected runtime exception — e.g. `UncheckedIOException` from a provider or from a filesystem call —
+caught in `Cli.run` and reported as "internal error: `<class>`: `<message>`" rather than escaping as a raw
+stack trace; or the thread being interrupted mid-walk, reported as "interrupted" and aborting the range
+immediately instead of limping through every remaining commit with the interrupt flag still set — 3 at least
+one row is `CHANGED`. 2 wins over 3 when both apply in the same range.
```

#### Commit T — `test(tracer): make the group/other-write fixture umask-independent`

Why: not one of the originally triaged items — found while running this plan's own `bazel test //...` step
for Commit S. `scratchRootWithGroupOrOtherWriteIsAUsageError` (added in Commit P) created its insecure fixture
directory via `Files.createDirectory(scratch, PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(...rwxrwxr-x...))`, which
passed under Gradle's test JVM (inherits the shell's `umask 0002`) but failed under `bazel test` (sandboxed test
runs default to a stricter umask — confirmed by printing the actual resulting permissions under each runner:
Gradle produced the requested `rwxrwxr-x`, Bazel produced `rwxr-xr-x` with the group write bit silently masked
off). `PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute`'s mode is evidently still subject to the process umask for
directory creation on this platform, contrary to the common assumption that it bypasses it.

Fix: create the directory with default permissions, then set the exact mode with a separate
`Files.setPosixFilePermissions` call — an explicit `chmod`, which is not subject to umask.

This is a test-only fix to a fixture from an already-committed commit (P); the no-amend rule for this shared
branch means it lands as its own commit rather than folded back into P's.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliTest.java b/tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliTest.java
index a6d17b0..967b531 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliTest.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/test/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/CliTest.java
@@ -346,7 +346,12 @@ class CliTest {
     // Ownership by another user can't be faked without root (chown requires privilege this test doesn't have and
     // shouldn't need), so only the permission-bits branch of the privacy check is exercised here.
     @Test void scratchRootWithGroupOrOtherWriteIsAUsageError() throws Exception {
-        Files.createDirectory(scratch, PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(PosixFilePermissions.fromString("rwxrwxr-x")));
+        // setPosixFilePermissions (a separate chmod), not createDirectory's FileAttribute: the attribute's mode
+        // is still subject to the process umask on at least some platforms/sandboxes (e.g. Bazel's sandboxed test
+        // runs default to a stricter umask than an interactive shell), so asking for group write at creation time
+        // isn't reliable; an explicit chmod afterward is.
+        Files.createDirectory(scratch);
+        Files.setPosixFilePermissions(scratch, PosixFilePermissions.fromString("rwxrwxr-x"));
 
         int code = run(keys, impacts, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip");
```

```bash
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
test(tracer): make the group/other-write fixture umask-independent

scratchRootWithGroupOrOtherWriteIsAUsageError created its insecure
fixture directory via createDirectory's FileAttribute, whose mode
turned out to still be subject to the process umask for directory
creation on this platform: it passed under Gradle's test JVM but
failed under bazel test, whose sandboxed runs default to a stricter
umask that silently masked off the group write bit the test needed.
Set the exact mode with a separate chmod instead, which umask can't
touch.
EOF
```

to confirm the lockfile didn't drift.

#### Commit U — `fix(tracer): compare the scratch root's owner by uid, not user name`

Why: `checkScratchRootIsPrivate` compared `Files.getOwner(scratch).getName()` with `System.getProperty("user.name")`.
For a uid with no passwd entry (common with `docker run -u $(id -u)`), the JVM reports `user.name` as `"?"` while
`getOwner()` still resolves to the numeric uid — so the very first run creates the root fine, and every later run
on it fails with a false "not private to you" (reproduced by re-review).

Before writing this, confirmed `com.sun.security.auth.module.UnixSystem` is not deprecated and raises no warning
under either toolchain: `javap`/reflection show no `@Deprecated` on the class or `getUid()`; `javac -Xlint:all`
is silent; `jdk.security.auth` unconditionally exports `com.sun.security.auth.module` (`java --describe-module
jdk.security.auth`), so no `--add-exports` is needed; `bazel build //tools/tracer:tracer_lib` and
`bazel test //tools/tracer:CliTest` both compile and pass clean under the `remotejdk_21` toolchain.

Fix: compare uids — `Files.getAttribute(scratch, "unix:uid")` (an `Integer`) against `new
UnixSystem().getUid()` (a `long`) — instead of names. Added a package-private `Cli` constructor overload taking
a `LongSupplier currentUid` (the public one defaults to `() -> new UnixSystem().getUid()`), so the
owner-mismatch branch finally has a test: a fake supplier that can never match the real file's uid, no root
needed.

#### Commit V — `fix(tracer): do not follow a symlinked scratch root`

Why: `checkScratchRootIsPrivate` read `scratch`'s permissions and owner following symlinks, and
`ensurePrivateScratchRoot` only recognised an existing *directory*. A scratch root that was itself a symlink —
to another user's directory, say — either fell through to the "doesn't exist yet" branch (failing with a
generic IO error for a symlink-to-file, or worse, silently trusting a symlink-to-directory's target) instead of
being rejected the way an insecure real directory is.

Fix: route a symlink through the same privacy check as a real directory (`Files.isDirectory(scratch,
NOFOLLOW_LINKS) || Files.isSymbolicLink(scratch)`), and make every read inside that check — permissions, owner
— pass `LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS`, so it inspects the link itself, never its target. On Linux a symlink's own
permission bits report as world read/write/execute no matter who created it, so this alone rejects any
symlink with the same "not private" message, without a separate symlink-specific branch.

```diff
+        // treated as "doesn't exist yet" and recreated or (for a symlink to a file) fail as a generic IO error.
+        if (Files.isDirectory(scratch, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS) || Files.isSymbolicLink(scratch)) {

+            long owner = ((Number) Files.getAttribute(scratch, "unix:uid", LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)).longValue();
```

#### Commit W — `fix(tracer): keep the --tsv-out file's mode and write through a symlink`

Why: after Commit M, every write went through `Files.createTempFile` (always `0600`) then an atomic move, which
discards the old target's inode and keeps the temp file's own mode — so an existing, more permissive target
(e.g. `0644`) silently became `0600` on every run. Separately, moving onto `--tsv-out`'s own path when it's a
symlink replaces the symlink's directory entry itself, turning it into a plain file instead of writing through
it to whatever it pointed at.

Fix: if the target exists, resolve it with `toRealPath()` first and write/move next to the *real* target, and
copy its existing POSIX permissions onto the temp file before the move. If the target is new, skip
`createTempFile` entirely — write through `Files.newOutputStream(tmp, CREATE_NEW, WRITE)` under a unique
sibling name instead, so the new file's mode follows the umask like any other file this tool creates, rather
than being pinned to `0600`. Cleanup of the temp file on failure is unchanged.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
index 42812f3..868839c 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
@@ -2,10 +2,12 @@ package io.joeyang.tracer.cli;
 
 import com.sun.security.auth.module.UnixSystem;
 import java.io.IOException;
+import java.io.OutputStream;
 import java.io.PrintStream;
 import java.nio.channels.FileChannel;
 import java.nio.channels.FileLock;
 import java.nio.channels.OverlappingFileLockException;
+import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
 import java.nio.file.Files;
 import java.nio.file.LinkOption;
 import java.nio.file.Path;
@@ -456,24 +458,47 @@ public final class Cli {
      * corrupted — the previous file, if any, is untouched.
      */
     private void writeTsvAtomically(Path target, String content) throws ProviderException {
-        Path parent = target.toAbsolutePath().getParent();
-        if (parent == null) {
-            parent = Path.of("").toAbsolutePath();
-        }
-        Path tmp;
+        // toRealPath, not target itself: a symlinked --tsv-out must have its real target replaced, not the
+        // symlink's own directory entry (which is what an atomic move onto the symlink's path would replace).
+        Path realTarget = target;
+        Set<PosixFilePermission> previousPermissions = null;
         try {
-            tmp = Files.createTempFile(parent, "trace-", ".tsv.tmp");
+            if (Files.exists(target)) {
+                realTarget = target.toRealPath();
+                previousPermissions = Files.getPosixFilePermissions(realTarget);
+            }
         } catch (IOException e) {
-            throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + target + ": " + ioReason(e, parent), e);
+            throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + target + ": " + ioReason(e, target), e);
+        }
+        Path parent = realTarget.toAbsolutePath().getParent();
+        if (parent == null) {
+            parent = Path.of("").toAbsolutePath();
         }
+        Path tmp = null;
         try {
-            Files.writeString(tmp, content);
-            Files.move(tmp, target, StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);
+            if (previousPermissions != null) {
+                // An existing target's mode must survive the replacement: createTempFile's own (always 0600)
+                // would otherwise silently become the new mode once the move discards the old inode.
+                tmp = Files.createTempFile(parent, "trace-", ".tsv.tmp");
+                Files.writeString(tmp, content);
+                Files.setPosixFilePermissions(tmp, previousPermissions);
+            } else {
+                // A brand-new file has no previous mode to preserve, so it's written plainly (mode follows the
+                // umask like any other file this tool creates) rather than through createTempFile, which is
+                // always 0600 regardless of umask.
+                tmp = parent.resolve(realTarget.getFileName() + ".tmp" + System.nanoTime());
+                try (OutputStream newFile = Files.newOutputStream(tmp, StandardOpenOption.CREATE_NEW, StandardOpenOption.WRITE)) {
+                    newFile.write(content.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
+                }
+            }
+            Files.move(tmp, realTarget, StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);
         } catch (IOException e) {
-            try {
-                Files.deleteIfExists(tmp);
-            } catch (IOException ignored) {
-                // best effort
+            if (tmp != null) {
+                try {
+                    Files.deleteIfExists(tmp);
+                } catch (IOException ignored) {
+                    // best effort
+                }
             }
             throw new ProviderException("cannot write " + target + ": " + ioReason(e, target), e);
         }
```

#### Commit X — `fix(tracer): report the filesystem reason in IO errors and skip usage for a non-private root`

Why: `ioReason` fell back to the exception's class name only when `getMessage()` was null or exactly equal to
the path; a `FileSystemException`'s `getMessage()` usually isn't exactly that path, so the fallback never
triggered where it most mattered — `".lock: Is a directory"` printed as `"...lock: .../.lock: Is a directory"`
(the path doubled), and a `--tsv-out` write failure printed the *temp file's* path as if it were a reason,
rather than the real target's. Separately, a non-private scratch root's rejection went through the generic
`usage()` helper, printing the full usage block for something that, like lock contention after Commit O, isn't
a command-line mistake.

Fix: for a `FileSystemException`, prefer its structured `getReason()` over `getMessage()`, falling back to the
class name only when `getReason()` itself is null. `checkScratchRootIsPrivate`/`ensurePrivateScratchRoot` now
print their own message and return a boolean instead of throwing `UsageException`, so `withScratchLock` can
return `EXIT_USAGE` directly — the same shape as lock contention's existing special case.

```diff

         Path lockPath = scratch.resolve(".lock");

             FileLock lock;
@@ -298,13 +301,13 @@ public final class Cli {

      * the current user with no group/other write bit, or the run refuses to touch it.
      */

+    private boolean ensurePrivateScratchRoot(PrintStream err) throws ProviderException {
         // isSymbolicLink alongside isDirectory: a symlinked scratch root must go through the privacy check too
         // (which reads the link's own attributes, not its target's — see checkScratchRootIsPrivate), not get
         // treated as "doesn't exist yet" and recreated or (for a symlink to a file) fail as a generic IO error.
         if (Files.isDirectory(scratch, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS) || Files.isSymbolicLink(scratch)) {
-            checkScratchRootIsPrivate();

@@ -315,9 +318,10 @@ public final class Cli {
         } catch (IOException e) {
             throw new ProviderException("cannot create scratch root " + scratch + ": " + ioReason(e, scratch), e);
         }
+        return true;
     }
 
-    private void checkScratchRootIsPrivate() throws UsageException, ProviderException {
+    private boolean checkScratchRootIsPrivate(PrintStream err) throws ProviderException {
         try {
             // NOFOLLOW_LINKS throughout: a symlinked scratch root is checked on the link itself, never on
             // whatever it points to. On Linux a symlink's own permissions report as world-read/write/execute
@@ -332,9 +336,13 @@ public final class Cli {
             long owner = ((Number) Files.getAttribute(scratch, "unix:uid", LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)).longValue();
             boolean notOwnedByMe = owner != currentUid.getAsLong();
             if (groupOrOtherWrite || notOwnedByMe) {
-                throw new UsageException("scratch root " + scratch

     private static String ioReason(IOException e, Path path) {
+        // FileSystemException's own getMessage() typically repeats the path we're already naming ("<path>: Is a
+        // directory", or for some failures just "<path>" with no reason at all), so prefer its structured
+        // getReason() first; a plain IOException's getMessage() doesn't have this problem.
```

```diff
 ### The scratch root must be private
 `$TMPDIR/gradle-tracer` is a predictable, fixed path (spec §9), often under a shared `/tmp`: another local user
 could pre-create it and tamper with the worktrees whose `gradlew` this tool executes, or hold `.lock` to block
 every run. `Cli` creates a fresh root `rwx------`; an existing one must already be owned by the current user
 with no group or other write permission, or the run refuses to touch it — exit 1, naming the path ("scratch
-root `<path>` is not private to you; remove it or set `TMPDIR` to a private directory") — before any sweep,
```

```bash
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
fix(tracer): report the filesystem reason in IO errors and skip usage for a non-private root

ioReason's fallback to the exception's class name only triggered when
getMessage() was exactly the path, which a FileSystemException's
message rarely is — so ".lock: Is a directory" printed with the path
doubled, and a --tsv-out write failure showed the temp file's path as
if it were the reason. Prefer FileSystemException.getReason() first.
Also: a non-private scratch root now prints just its own message and
returns EXIT_USAGE directly, instead of going through the full usage
block, the same as lock contention's existing special case.
EOF
```

---

#### Commit Y — `fix(tracer): check for interrupts at the top of each commit`

Why: Commit N only checked `Thread.currentThread().isInterrupted()` after a failed *key* lookup. An
interrupted bazel-diff call degrades silently — like any other bazel-diff failure, it becomes `NOT_AVAILABLE`
with a warning, not an abort — so the flag could stay set going into the next commit with nothing having
reported "interrupted" yet. That next commit's own checkout (or anything else needing a process) would then
fail for an unrelated-looking reason, with the real cause (the interrupt) never surfaced.

Fix: check the flag at the top of every iteration too, before that commit's checkout or any of its provider
calls, aborting with the same `ProviderException("interrupted")` path N already added.

```diff
diff --git a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
index f354e7a..7b12b70 100644
--- a/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
+++ b/tools/tracer/src/main/java/io/joeyang/tracer/cli/Cli.java
@@ -404,6 +404,15 @@ public final class Cli {
                 Path previousHashes = null;
                 boolean bazelAlive = impacts != null;
                 for (Commit commit : commits) {
+                    // Checked here too, not only after a failed key lookup: an interrupted bazel-diff call
+                    // degrades silently (as an ordinary bazel-diff failure, below), so the flag can still be set
+                    // going into the next commit even though nothing in the previous iteration reported it as
+                    // "interrupted". Catching it here, before this commit's checkout or any of its provider
+                    // calls, is what stops it reaching the next checkout and failing there with a confusing
+                    // unrelated error instead.
+                    if (Thread.currentThread().isInterrupted()) {
+                        throw new ProviderException("interrupted");
+                    }
                     Path worktree = pool.checkout(commit);
                     Optional<TaskKey> key = Optional.empty();
                     Optional<String> error = Optional.empty();
```

```diff
@@ -510,6 +510,30 @@ class CliTest {
         }
     }
 
+    @Test void interruptedBazelDiffAbortsTheWalkAtTheNextCommit() throws Exception {
+        java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger hashCalls = new java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger();
+        ImpactProvider interrupting = new ImpactProvider() {
+            @Override public Path hashes(Path worktree, Path output) throws ProviderException {
+                hashCalls.incrementAndGet();
+                Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
+                throw new ProviderException("boom");
+            }
+            @Override public Set<String> impactedTargets(Path p, Path c) { return Set.of(); }
+        };
+        try {
+            int code = run(keys, interrupting, "appA", "--from", "base", "--to", "tip");
+
+            assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_TOOL_FAILURE, code, err());
+            assertTrue(err().contains("interrupted"), err());
+            // The interrupted hashes() call itself degrades (silently, as a normal bazel-diff failure); the
+            // walk only stops at the top of the *next* iteration, before that commit's checkout or any of its
+            // provider calls.
+            assertEquals(1, hashCalls.get(), "no further bazel-diff calls once the flag is seen");
+        } finally {
+            Thread.interrupted();
+        }
+    }
+
     @Test void staleScratchFilesAreSweptAndNewOnesRemoved() throws Exception {
         createPrivateScratch();
         Path stale = Files.writeString(scratch.resolve("hashes-stale.json"), "{}");
```
